, and choosing first
\delta
sufficiently small and then
m
sufficiently large depending on
\delta
, we obtain functions
in
\mathrm{span}\{e_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
converging to
f
in
H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}
, which shows the claim.

Analogously, taking the limit as
m\to\infty
in (
3.24
), according to (
3.26
), one has

(3.27)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left<\phi_{0}^{2}w_{t},\Phi\right>_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\phi_{0}P_{1},\Phi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<P_{2},\Phi\right>\,\mathrm{d}t,

for all
\Phi\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}})
. Finally, choosing
\Phi(t,x)=\varphi(x)\in H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}
and applying
\partial_{t}
to both sides of (
3.27
), one can show that the weak formulation (
3.5
) holds.

Step 4: Uniqueness and time continuity.

Since
w\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}})
, it follows from (
3.26
) and Lemma
A.7
that

(3.28)

\phi_{0}w\in C([0,T];L^{2}).

It remains to show that
w(0,x)=w_{0}
for a.e.
x\in I
. On the one hand, thanks to (
3.5
) and (
3.28
), for any
\Phi\in C_{c}^{1}([0,T);H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}})
, it holds that

(3.29)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\left<\Phi_{t},\phi_{0}^{2}w\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t-K_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi\right>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{0}^{2}w(0),\Phi(0)\right>+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\phi_{0}P_{1},\Phi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<P_{2},\Phi\right>\,\mathrm{d}t.

On the other hand, choosing
\Phi^{m}(t,x)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\xi_{j}(t)e_{j}
,
\xi(t)\in C^{\infty}_{c}[0,T)
satisfying
\Phi^{m}\to\Phi
in
C^{1}_{c}([0,T);H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}})
, as
m\to\infty
, one gets from (
3.23
) that for all
m\leq n
,

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\big<\Phi^{m}_{t},\phi_{0}^{2}X^{n,\delta}\big>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\bigg<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}X^{n,\delta}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi^{m}_{x}\bigg>\,\mathrm{d}t-K_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\bigg<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}X^{n,\delta}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi^{m}\bigg>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big<\phi_{0}^{2}X^{n,\delta}(0),\Phi^{m}(0)\big>+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\phi_{0}P_{1},\Phi^{m}_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<P_{2},\Phi^{m}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t,

which, by first letting
n,\delta^{-1}\to\infty
along the subsequence in (
3.22
) for each fixed
m
and then letting
m\to\infty
, leads to

(3.30)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\left<\Phi_{t},\phi_{0}^{2}w\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t-K_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\Phi\right>\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{0}^{2}w_{0},\Phi(0)\right>+\int_{0}^{T}\left<\phi_{0}P_{1},\Phi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{0}^{T}\left<P_{2},\Phi\right>\,\mathrm{d}t.

It follows from (
3.29
)-(
3.30
) that
w(0,x)=w_{0}
for a.e.
x\in I
. Finally, setting
w_{0}=0
,
\varphi=w
and
P_{1}=P_{2}=0
, one gets easily that
w=0
, which implies the uniqueness.

The proof of Proposition
3.1
is completed.
∎

Remark 3.1
.

(
3.27
) and (
3.29
) are equivalent to (
3.5
) (see Chapter 7 in
[
15
]
for details).

3.2.2.
Case
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1

In this case, we consider the following initial boundary value problem:

(3.31)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}w_{t}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}=-\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}P_{3}\big)_{x}&\text{in }(0,T]\times I,\\
w=w_{0}&\text{on }\{t=0\}\times I,\end{cases}

where
P_{3}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
and
w_{0}\in L^{2}_{\phi_{0}^{1/\alpha}}
,
and the definition of weak solutions of this problem can be given as follows.

Definition 3.2
.

A function
w(t,x)
is said to be a weak solution in
[0,T]\times I
, to (
3.31
), if

\mathrm{i)}

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}w\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}w_{x}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}w_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{1/\alpha}})
;

\mathrm{ii)}

the following equation holds for all
\varphi\in H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{1/\alpha}}
and a.e. time
0<t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\big<\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}w_{t},\varphi\big>_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{1/\alpha}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{1/\alpha}}}+\bigg<\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}w_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\bigg>=\big<\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}P_{3},\varphi_{x}\big>;

\mathrm{iii)}

w(0,x)=w_{0}(x)
for a.e.
x\in I
.

Observe that one can set
K_{0}=P_{2}=0
and replace
(\phi_{0},P_{1})\mapsto(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}},P_{3})
in (
3.4
) to obtain (
3.31
). Compared with
\eqref{galerkin-w}_{1}
,
\eqref{galerkin-w'}_{1}
takes a simpler form since such terms as
K_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{-2}\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w_{x}
and
P_{2}
in
\eqref{galerkin-w}_{1}
do not appear in
\eqref{galerkin-w'}_{1}
. Thus, following the proof of Proposition
3.1
with
K_{0}=P_{2}=0
and
(\phi_{0},P_{1})
replaced by
(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}},P_{3})
, one can get the following result.

Proposition 3.2
.

There exists a unique weak solution
w
to problem (
3.31
), satisfying the following estimate: for all
0<T\leq\widetilde{T}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}w\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}w_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}+\big\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}w_{t}\big\|_{-1,\phi_{0}^{1/\alpha}}^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}w_{0}\big|_{2}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{T}\left|P_{3}\right|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t.

3.3.
Proof of Lemma
3.1
when
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}

Now, we start to prove Lemma
3.1
. The proof for the case
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
is given by the following several steps.

Proof.

Step 1: Tangential estimate
\phi_{0}U\in C([0,T];L^{2})
.
Turn back to (
3.1
) and let

\begin{split}\quad w_{0}^{(0)}:=&u_{0},\ \ K_{0}:=\frac{1}{\alpha}-2>0,\ \ P_{1}^{(0)}:=\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{-2},\ \ P_{2}^{(0)}:=\Big(2-\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{-2}.\end{split}

It is clear that
P_{1}^{(0)},P_{2}^{(0)}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
and
w_{0}^{(0)}\in L^{2}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}
. Then it follows from Proposition
3.1
that there exists a unique weak solution
w^{(0)}=U
satisfying

(3.32)

\phi_{0}U\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}U_{x}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}).

Step 2: Tangential estimates
\phi_{0}U_{x},\phi_{0}U_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2})
.

Applying
\partial_{t}
to both sides of
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
formally, one has

(3.33)

\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tt}-\left(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\big(\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(1)}\big)_{x}+P_{2}^{(1)},

where

\displaystyle P_{1}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}},

\displaystyle P_{2}^{(1)}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\bigg(\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\bigg).

Then regard (
3.33
) as the equation of
w^{(1)}:=U_{t}
and consider the problem

(3.34)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\quad\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(1)}_{t}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(1)}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}\\[8.0pt]
\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w^{(1)}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\big(\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(1)}\big)_{x}+P_{2}^{(1)}&\text{in }(0,T]\times I,\\[4.0pt]
\quad w^{(1)}=U_{t}(0,x)&\text{on }\{t=0\}\times I.\end{cases}

Similarly, it follows from (
3.32
) that
w^{(1)}(0,x)\in L^{2}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}
and
P_{1}^{(1)},P_{2}^{(1)}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
. Consequently, by Proposition
3.1
, the weak solution
w^{(1)}
to (
3.34
) exists uniquely satisfying

(3.35)

\phi_{0}w^{(1)}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}w^{(1)}_{x}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(1)}_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}).

Now, we check that
w^{(1)}=U_{t}
. Indeed, since
U
and
w^{(1)}
are weak solutions to the problems (
3.1
) and (
3.34
), respectively, one gets from (
3.5
) that for all
\varphi\in H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}
and a.e. time
0<t\leq T
,

(3.36)

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t},\varphi\right>_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}}+\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\right>+\big<\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(0)},\varphi_{x}\big>+\big<P_{2}^{(0)},\varphi\big>,

and

(3.37)

\displaystyle\big<\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(1)}_{t},\varphi\big>_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}}+\bigg<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(1)}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\bigg>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\bigg<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w^{(1)}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\bigg>+\big<\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(1)},\varphi_{x}\big>+\big<P_{2}^{(1)},\varphi\big>.

Next, define

W(t,x):=\int_{0}^{t}w^{(1)}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s+u_{0}(x)\ \ \text{and}\ \ Y:=W-U.

It suffices to show that
Y=0
pointwise. To get this, substituting
W
into (
3.37
) and integrating the resulting equality over
[0,t]
for
0<t\leq T
, then according to the compatibility condition
\eqref{1..16}_{1}
, one has

\displaystyle\big<\phi_{0}^{2}W_{t},\varphi\big>+\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}W_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\right>-\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}W_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\left<\frac{2\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{U}_{x}Y_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}},\varphi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}s+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\left<\frac{2\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}Y_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}},\varphi\right>\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\big<\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(0)},\varphi_{x}\big>+\big<P_{2}^{(0)},\varphi\big>,

which, together with (
3.36
), leads to

(3.38)

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{0}^{2}Y_{t},\varphi\right>_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}}+\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}Y_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\right>-\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}Y_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\right>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\left<\frac{2\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{U}_{x}Y_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}},\varphi_{x}\right>\,\mathrm{d}s+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\left<\frac{2\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}Y_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}},\varphi\right>\,\mathrm{d}s.

Since
Y\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}})
, one may set
\varphi=Y
in (
3.38
). Then it follows from the same calculations in (
3.14
), Lemma
A.5
and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\phi_{0}Y|_{2}^{2}+|\phi_{0}Y_{x}|_{2}^{2}\leq C|\phi_{0}Y|_{2}^{2}+C(|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty})\int_{0}^{t}\big(|\phi_{0}Y|_{2}^{2}+|\phi_{0}Y_{x}|_{2}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, along with the strong continuity of
Y
at
t=0
,
Y|_{t=0}=0
and Grönwall’s inequality, implies that for all
0<T\leq\widetilde{T}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|\phi_{0}Y\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi_{0}Y_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq C(|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty})Te^{CT}\Big(T\sup_{s\in[0,T]}\left|\phi_{0}Y\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\left|\phi_{0}Y_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\Big).

Note that, by choosing
0<T_{0}<1
such that
C(|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty})T_{0}e^{CT_{0}}=\frac{1}{2}
and using Lemma
A.5
, one can obtain from the estimates above that
Y=0
a.e. on
(0,T_{0})\times I
. Since
T_{0}
depends only on
\alpha
and
(\phi_{0},\bar{U})
, one can extend
T_{0}
to
\widetilde{T}
via the analogous arguments in (
3.19
)-(
3.21
), which yields
Y=0
a.e. on
(0,\widetilde{T})\times I
.

As a consequence,
U_{t}
satisfies (
3.35
), that is,

(3.39)

\phi_{0}U_{t}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}U_{tx}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tt}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}),

which, along with (
3.32
), yields that

(3.40)

\phi_{0}U_{x}\in C([0,T];L^{2}).

Step 3: Boundary condition of
U
.
By (
3.1
) and (
3.5
), one has the following lemma.

Lemma 3.4
.

It holds that

(3.41)

\left(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}\in C([0,T];L^{2}).

Furthermore, the equation
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
holds for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
, and
U
satisfies

(3.42)

\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}=0\ \ \text{for }x\in\Gamma.

Proof.

Indeed, it follows from (
3.5
) with
U
that for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}
,

\displaystyle\Big|-\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\right>\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\left<\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t},\varphi\right>-\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\right>-\big<\big(\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(0)}\big)_{x},\varphi\big>+\big<P_{2}^{(0)},\varphi\big>\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(1+\left\|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right\|_{C_{t}(L^{2})}+\left\|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right\|_{C_{t}(L^{2})}\big)\left|\varphi\right|_{2}\leq C\left|\varphi\right|_{2},

which means that
\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{-2}U_{x}
admits the weak derivative
\left(\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{-2}U_{x}\right)_{x}\in L^{2}
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
. In addition, due to the time-independent bound in the right hand side of the above inequality and the time continuity of
(\phi_{0}U_{x},\phi_{0}U_{t},\bar{\eta})
, one obtains (
3.41
). Besides, it follows from (
3.39
)-(
3.41
) that
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
holds for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
.

It remains to prove (
3.42
). On the one hand, thanks to the weak formulation (
3.5
), it holds that for all
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
,

(3.43)

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t},\varphi\rangle+\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\right>=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\right>+\big<\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(0)},\varphi_{x}\big>+\big<P_{2}^{(0)},\varphi\big>;

on the other hand, it follows from Lemma
A.4
and (
3.41
) that
\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}\in C([0,T]\times\bar{I})
. Then multiplying both sides of
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by such
\varphi
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
lead to

(3.44)

\displaystyle\left<\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t},\varphi\right>+\left<\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi_{x}\right>-\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\varphi\Big|_{x=0}^{x=1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\left<\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},\varphi\right>+\big<\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(0)},\varphi_{x}\big>+\big<P_{2}^{(0)},\varphi\big>.

Comparing with (
3.43
)-(
3.44
) and using
\frac{1}{2}\leq\bar{\eta}_{x}\leq\frac{3}{2}
for
(t,x)\in[0,\widetilde{T}]\times\bar{I}
, one gets

\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}\varphi\big|_{x=0}^{x=1}=0
for all
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
,
which yields that
\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}|_{\Gamma}=0
.

The proof of Lemma
3.4
is completed.
∎

Step 4: Tangential estimates
\phi_{0}U_{tx},\phi_{0}U_{tt}\in C([0,T];L^{2})
.

We start with the following claim:

(3.45)

U_{x}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{\infty}).

Indeed, on the one hand, based on
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
and Lemmas
D.1
-
D.2
, one can reformulate
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by multiplying both of its sides by
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}
to deduce that

(3.46)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{t}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}+\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}=0;

on the other hand, since
\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}\in C(\bar{I})
, it follows from integrating (
3.46
) over
[0,x]
,
0<x\leq\frac{1}{2}
, (
3.39
), Lemma
A.5
,
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
,
\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
and Hölder’s inequality that

(3.47)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{x}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}+\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\int_{0}^{x}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\implies\left|U_{x}(t,x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq Cx^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}+Cx^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|U_{t}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}(x)+C\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(x)\left(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\right).

For
\frac{1}{2}<x\leq 1
, integrating (
3.46
) over
[x,1]
yields that
\eqref{3...136}_{2}
still holds. Thus, taking the square of
\eqref{3...136}_{2}
and integrating the resulting inequality over
[0,T]
shows the claim (
3.45
).

Now, we continue to improve the tangential regularities. Applying
\partial_{t}^{2}
to both sides of
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
yields formally that

(3.48)

\phi_{0}^{2}\partial_{t}^{3}U-\left(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\big(\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(2)}\big)_{x}+P_{2}^{(2)},

where

\displaystyle P_{1}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{tx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}+\frac{4\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}-\frac{6\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{4}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}+\frac{6\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{4}},

(3.49)

\displaystyle P_{2}^{(2)}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\bigg(\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}-\frac{6\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{4}}+\frac{6\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{4}}\bigg.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\frac{2\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{tx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}-\frac{4\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\right).

Then regard (
3.48
) as the equation of
w^{(2)}:=U_{tt}
and consider the problem

(3.50)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\quad\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(2)}_{t}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}w^{(2)}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}\\
\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}w^{(2)}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\big(\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(2)}\big)_{x}+P_{2}^{(2)}&\text{in }(0,T]\times I,\\
\quad w^{(2)}=U_{tt}(0,x)&\text{on }\{t=0\}\times I.\end{cases}

One can check from (
3.39
) and (
3.45
) that
P_{1}^{(2)},P_{2}^{(2)}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
,
w^{(2)}(0,x)\in L^{2}_{\phi_{0}^{2}}
. Consequently, it follows from Proposition
3.1
that there exists a unique weak solution
w^{(2)}
to (
3.50
), and based on the analogous arguments in
Step 2
, one has
w^{(2)}=U_{tt}
. Thus,

(3.51)

\phi_{0}U_{tt}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\in L^{2}([0,T];L^{2}).

Certainly, it follows from (
3.39
) and (
3.51
) that

(3.52)

\phi_{0}U_{tx}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

and one can deduce that
\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tx}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
by the similar discussions in Lemma
3.4
.

Then it follows from (
3.32
), (
3.39
)-(
3.40
) and (
3.51
)-(
3.52
) that

(3.53)

\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{j}U\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad j=0,1,2;\quad\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{j}U_{x}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad j=0,1.

Step 5: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
.

First, it follows from (
3.45
)-(
3.47
) and (
3.53
) that

(3.54)

U_{x}\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times\bar{I}).

Next, due to Lemma
D.1
, multiplying both sides of
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\phi_{0}^{-1}
yields

(3.55)

\phi_{0}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}=\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}+\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}+1}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}},

for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
. Then, it follows from (
3.53
) that the right hand side of (
3.55
) can be bounded in the norm of
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
. Consequently,

(3.56)

\phi_{0}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}),

which, together with (
3.54
), implies that

(3.57)

\phi_{0}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Step 6: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
.

First, we claim that

(3.58)

\phi_{0}^{\iota}U_{tx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})\ \ \text{for all }\iota>0.

Indeed, for
0<x\leq\frac{1}{2}
, applying
\partial_{t}
to both sides of
\eqref{3...136}_{1}
, one then gets from (
3.53
), Lemma
A.5
and Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tx}

\displaystyle=2\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{U}_{x}\int_{0}^{x}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}z+\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\int_{0}^{x}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\implies\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tx}(t,x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{x}z^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|U_{t}\right|\,\mathrm{d}z+\int_{0}^{x}z^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|U_{tt}\right|\,\mathrm{d}z\Big)

(3.59)

\displaystyle\leq Cx^{\frac{2-\alpha}{2\alpha}}\big(x\left|U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0}^{\frac{2-\alpha}{2\alpha}}\big(\phi_{0}(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2})+1\big)\leq C\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{2+\alpha}{2\alpha}}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{2-\alpha}{2\alpha}}\big).

Similar arguments can be applied to
[x,1]
,
\frac{1}{2}<x\leq 1
. Then, multiplying both sides of
\eqref{00439}_{2}
by
\phi_{0}^{\iota-\frac{1}{\alpha}}
and taking the
L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
norm of the resulting inequality give (
3.58
).

Next, since (
3.55
) holds pointwise, based on the estimates (
3.53
), the regularities of
(\bar{\eta},\bar{U})
and Lemma
D.2
, one can apply
\partial_{t}
to both sides of (
3.55
) to obtain that

(3.60)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}=

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{tt}+2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U_{t}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}.

Then, it follows from (
3.53
), (
3.60
) and Lemma
A.5
that

(3.61)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}),

which, together with (
3.58
) and (
3.61
) multiplied by
\phi_{0}^{\iota}
, yields
\phi_{0}^{1+\iota}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
, for all
\iota>0
. Hence, it follows from Proposition
C.1
in Appendix
C
that

(3.62)

\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Step 7: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
.

First, it follows from (
3.53
), (
3.57
) and Lemma
D.2
that one can apply
\partial_{x}
to (
3.55
) to obtain

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}

(3.63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{x}+\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{t}+(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}

\displaystyle-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-1}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}+\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}},

which, along with (
3.53
), (
3.57
) and Lemma
A.5
, yields that

(3.64)

\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Obviously, it follows from
\eqref{0442}\times\phi_{0}
and (
3.57
) that
\phi_{0}^{2}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
. Since
2<\frac{1}{2\alpha}+1
, one obtains from Proposition
C.1
and (
3.57
) that

(3.65)

\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Step 8: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
.

Analogously, based on (
3.53
), (
3.57
), (
3.65
) and Lemma
D.2
, one can apply
\partial_{x}
to both sides of (
3.63
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+2\left((\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}+2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}(\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}+\bar{\eta}_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta})U_{t}

\displaystyle+\left((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{x}+4(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}-\Big(\frac{2}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{xx}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}U_{x}+2\Big((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}+2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\Big)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

(3.66)

\displaystyle+2\phi_{0}\Big(\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\Big)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

\displaystyle+4\Big(\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}+(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}-\frac{\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\Big)\frac{U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}U}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-1}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}\frac{\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{(\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{2(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{3}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{3}+\frac{6}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}(\phi_{0})_{xx}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0},

which, along with (
3.53
), (
3.57
), (
3.62
), (
3.65
), Lemma
A.5
and
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, yields that

(3.67)

\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Similarly, one can get from
\eqref{0445}\times\phi_{0}
and (
3.65
) that
\phi_{0}^{2}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
, which, along with Proposition
C.1
and (
3.65
), leads to

(3.68)

\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

In summary, collecting (
3.57
), (
3.62
), (
3.65
) and (
3.68
) yields all the elliptic estimates:

(3.69)

\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}U,\,\,\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad j=2,3,4.

Step 9: Time continuity.
To this end, it suffices to show the time continuity of
\phi_{0}U_{xx}
. Note that the time continuity of
\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}U
(
j=3,4
) and
\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
can be proved analogously.

To see this, according to (
3.53
), (
3.55
), and the following regularities of
\bar{\eta}
,

(3.70)

\bar{\eta}\in C^{1}([0,T];H^{3})\cap C^{2}([0,T];H^{1}),\quad\bar{\eta}_{t}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T];E),

one can first show that

(3.71)

\widetilde{F}(t,x):=\phi_{0}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\in C([0,T];L^{2}).

Then, it follows from Proposition
C.1
that for any
t,t_{0}\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t)-\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t_{0})\right|_{2}\leq C|\widetilde{F}(t)-\widetilde{F}(t_{0})|_{2}+C\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}(t)-\phi_{0}U_{x}(t_{0})\right|_{2},

which, by letting
t\to t_{0}
, along with (
3.53
) and (
3.71
), yields

\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t)-\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t_{0})\right|_{2}\to 0.

Then one has
\phi_{0}U_{xx}\in C([0,T];L^{2})
.

For
\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
and
\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}U
(
j=3,4
), it follows from (
3.61
), (
3.64
), (
3.67
) and Proposition
C.1
that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in C([0,T];L^{2});

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in C([0,T];L^{2});

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in C([0,T];L^{2}).

These, together with the tangential estimates (
3.53
), show that
U\in\mathscr{C}([0,T];E)
, which, along with Lemma
A.5
, yields

(3.72)

U\in C([0,T];H^{3})\cap C^{1}([0,T];H^{1}).

Step 10: Derivation of Neumann boundary condition.

First, multiplying both sides of (
3.1
) by
\phi_{0}^{-1}
shows that

(3.73)

\phi_{0}U_{t}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}=\frac{\phi_{0}U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}.

Then, according to
\phi_{0}\in H^{3}
, (
3.70
), (
3.72
) and Lemma
A.4
, one has

(3.74)

\phi_{0}\in C^{2}(\bar{I}),\ \ \bar{\eta}\in C^{1}([0,T];C^{2}(\bar{I})),\ \ U\in C([0,T];C^{2}(\bar{I}))\cap C^{1}([0,T];C(\bar{I})),

which, by letting
x\to\Gamma
in (
3.73
), along with
\phi_{0}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
and
\frac{1}{2}\leq\bar{\eta}_{x}\leq\frac{3}{2}
, yields that

(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}=0\ \ \text{for }x\in\Gamma.

Since
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
and
(\phi_{0})_{x}|_{\Gamma}\neq 0
, one can obtain from the above equality that
U_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.1

\mathrm{i)}
is completed.
∎

3.4.
Proof of Lemma
3.1
when
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1

The proof of Lemma
3.1
when
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
can be done by following the analogous arguments used for the case
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
.

Proof.

Step 1: Tangential estimates of
U
.
Compare (
3.2
) with (
3.31
) and set
P_{3}
in (
3.31
) as

P_{3}:=\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{-2}
.
Then, by Proposition
3.2
, the proof of this part can be done by exactly following that of
Step 1
-
Step 4
in §
3.3
. After the repetitive calculation, one can deduce that (
3.2
) admits a unique weak solution
U
satisfying the tangential estimates

(3.75)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\partial_{t}^{j}U\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad j=0,1,2;\quad\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\partial_{t}^{j}U_{x}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\quad j=0,1.

Moreover,
U
satisfies the equation
\eqref{lp}_{1}
for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
, and

(3.76)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{x}=\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tx}=0\ \ \text{for }x\in\Gamma.

Step 2: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}
.

First, following the arguments in (
3.47
) and integrating
\eqref{lp}_{1}
over
[0,x]
for
x\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right]
(or
[x,1]
for
x\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right]
), one can deduce from (
3.75
), Lemma
A.5
and Hölder’s inequality that, for
\iota>0
,

(3.77)

\displaystyle\left|U_{x}(t,x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}+C\phi_{0}^{\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}-\frac{\iota}{2}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\frac{\iota}{2}}U_{t}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}+C(\iota)\phi_{0}^{\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}-\frac{\iota}{2}}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}\big)\leq C\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}-\frac{\iota}{2}}\big).

Multiplying both sides of (
3.77
) by
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2+\iota}
, along with
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
, one gets

(3.78)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2+\iota}U_{x}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})\ \ \text{for all }\iota>0.

Next, according to Lemma
D.1
and multiplying both sides of
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}
, one has that for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
,

(3.79)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}.

Since
\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\geq\frac{1}{2\alpha}
(
\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{2}
for
\alpha=1
), it follows from (
3.75
) that

(3.80)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Therefore, letting
\iota=\frac{5}{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}
, then (
3.78
) and (
3.80
) imply that

(3.81)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Step 3: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
.

First, following the arguments in (
3.59
), integrating
\eqref{lp}_{1}
over
[0,x]
for
x\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}\right]
(or
[x,1]
for
x\in\left(\frac{1}{2},1\right]
) and then applying
\partial_{t}
to the resulting identity, one can get from (
3.75
), Lemma
A.5
and Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tx}(t,x)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{x}z^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|U_{t}\right|\,\mathrm{d}z+C\int_{0}^{x}z^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|U_{tt}\right|\,\mathrm{d}z

(3.82)

\displaystyle\leq Cx^{\frac{1+3\alpha}{2\alpha}-\frac{\varsigma}{2}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\frac{\varsigma}{2}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+Cx^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varsigma)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+3\alpha}{2\alpha}-\frac{\varsigma}{2}}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}\big)+C\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varsigma)\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+3\alpha}{2\alpha}-\frac{\varsigma}{2}}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2\alpha}}\big),

where
\varsigma\in(0,1)
, which multiplied by
\phi_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\iota}
(
\iota>0
), along with
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
, yields

(3.83)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\iota}U_{tx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}),\quad\text{for all }\iota>0.

Next, applying
\partial_{t}
to both sides of (
3.79
) and using Lemma
D.1
, one has

(3.84)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{tt}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U_{t}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}},

which, together with the fact that
\varepsilon_{0}\in\left(0,\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}\right]
(or
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1)
for
\alpha=1
) and (
3.75
), implies that

(3.85)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Hence for
\varepsilon_{0}\in\left(0,\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}\right)
(
\alpha\in\left(\frac{1}{3},1\right]
), setting
\iota:=\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}
in (
3.83
) and using (
3.85
), one has

(3.86)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2});

for
\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}
(
\alpha\in\left(\frac{1}{3},1\right)
), one can multiply (
3.85
) by
\phi_{0}^{\iota}
and deduce from (
3.83
) that
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}+\iota}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
, which, along with Proposition
C.1
and (
3.75
), yields (
3.86
).

Step 4: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U
.

First, multiplying
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\phi_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}}
and applying
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}
to the resulting equality, which can be justified due to Lemmas
D.1
-
D.2
, one gets that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{x}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{t}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}

(3.87)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}.

Then, it follows from (
3.75
), (
3.81
) and Lemma
A.5
that

(3.88)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Due to Proposition
C.1
, it remains to verify
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+1}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
. Indeed, by (
3.75
), one can reduce the power of weights in (
3.80
) from
\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}
to
\frac{1}{2\alpha}
, and obtain that

(3.89)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}),

which, along with (
3.78
), leads to

(3.90)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Then,
\eqref{3...152}\times\phi_{0}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}}
and (
3.90
) imply that
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+1}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
.

Hence, it follows from Proposition
C.1
and (
3.81
) that

(3.91)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Step 5: Elliptic estimate
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U
.

Analogously, it follows from multiplying
\eqref{lp}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\phi_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{\alpha}}
and applying
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{2}
to the resulting equality (due to Lemmas
D.1
-
D.2
) that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left((\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}+2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left(\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}+\bar{\eta}_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\right)U_{t}

\displaystyle+\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{x}+4(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}-\Big(\frac{2}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{xx}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}U_{x}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}+2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\Big)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

(3.92)

\displaystyle+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\Big)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

\displaystyle+4\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}+(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}-\frac{\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\Big)\frac{U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}U}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}\frac{\phi_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\frac{(\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle+\boxed{\frac{2(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{3}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{3}}+\frac{6}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}(\phi_{0})_{xx}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}.

It is worth noting that the framed term in (
3.92
) vanishes whenever
\alpha=1
.

Next, since
\varepsilon_{0}
satisfies (
1.18
), it follows from (
3.75
), (
3.81
), (
3.86
), (
3.91
)-(
3.92
) and Lemma
A.5
that

(3.93)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

In order to use Proposition
C.1
, it remains to check that
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
. To this end, according to (
3.75
), Lemma
A.5
and the assumption that
\alpha\leq 1
, one can change the power of weights in (
3.88
) from
\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}
to
\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}
, and obtain that

(3.94)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

Since one has already shown that
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{xx},\,\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+1}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
in
Step 4
, it follows from (
3.94
) and Proposition
C.1
that

(3.95)

\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2}).

As a consequence, multiplying (
3.93
) by
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\varepsilon_{0}}
, one can obtain from (
3.95
) that
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
. Then according to Proposition
C.1
, (
3.91
) and (
3.93
), one has

\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in L^{\infty}([0,T];L^{2})
.

Step 6: Time continuity.
Following the proof of
Step 9
in the first case, one can check the continuities of (
3.80
), (
3.85
), (
3.88
) and (
3.93
) in turn, and then make use of (
3.75
) and Proposition
C.1
to show that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in C([0,T];L^{2}),

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\in C([0,T];L^{2})

\displaystyle\implies\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U\in C([0,T];L^{2}).

Here, it should be mentioned that, when establishing the continuities of
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}
and
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
, the parameters
(\varepsilon_{0},\alpha)
do not satisfy the constraints imposed in Proposition
C.1
unless
\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}
. Fortunately, when
\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{2\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}
, the weight
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}
gains higher-order powers so that we can obtain their continuities directly from (
3.79
) and (
3.84
); for example, to derive the time-continuity of
\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{tx}
on the left-hand side of (
3.84
), we can apply an argument similar to (
3.82
)–(
3.83
) since the power
\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}
, which is larger than
\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\iota
for sufficiently small
\iota>0
given in (
3.83
).

Collecting all these estimates, together with (
3.75
), shows that
U\in\mathscr{C}([0,T];\widetilde{E})
, which, along with Lemma
A.5
, yields
U\in C([0,T];W^{3,1})\cap C^{1}([0,T];W^{1,1})
.

Step 7: Derivation of the Neumann boundary condition.
One can deduce from Lemma
A.4
that (
3.74
) holds and then follow the same proof of
Step 10
in §
3.3
to show that
U_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
.

Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.1

\mathrm{ii)}
is completed.
∎

4.
Uniform estimates to the linearized problems

With the help of Lemma
3.1
, in §
4.1
, we first consider the case for
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
and give a specific derivation of the uniform estimates on the classical solution
U
to the linearized problem (
3.1
). The proof for the case
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
is basically the same as the one for the case
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, and we only give a sketch in §
4.2
.

4.1.
Uniform estimates for the case
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume that
U
is the unique classical solution in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
to the problem (
3.1
) obtained in Lemma
3.1
, and the positive constant
c_{0}
satisfies

(4.1)

2+\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}+E(0,U)\leq c_{0}.

Then there exist a positive time
T_{*}\in(0,T]
and constants
c_{i}
,
i=1,2
,
1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}\leq c_{2}
, which depend only on
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
|I|
,
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, such that if for all
0\leq t\leq T_{*}
,

(4.2)

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}\bar{U}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{t}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{xx}(t)|_{2}\leq c_{1},

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{tx}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{3}_{x}\bar{U}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{tt}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}\bar{U}_{xx}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{4}_{x}\bar{U}(t)|_{2}\leq c_{2},

it holds that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{*}
,

(4.3)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}\leq c_{1},

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}(t)\right|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{3}_{x}U(t)|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{4}_{x}U(t)|_{2}\leq c_{2}.

4.1.1.
Basic estimates

First, according to the assumption (
4.2
) and Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
, it holds that for all
0\leq t\leq T
,

(4.4)

\begin{split}|\bar{U}_{x}(t)|_{2}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{2}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}(t)|_{2}\leq&Cc_{1},\\
\|\bar{U}_{x}(t)\|_{1,\infty}\leq C\|\bar{U}_{x}(t)\|_{2}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{4}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}(t)|_{2}\leq&Cc_{2},\\
\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{U}_{tx}(t)\big|_{\infty}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{2}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}_{t}(t)|_{2}\leq&Cc_{2}.\end{split}

Next, there exists a positive time
\widetilde{T}>0
, such that

(4.5)

\frac{1}{2}\leq\bar{\eta}_{x}(t,x)\leq\frac{3}{2}\ \ \text{for all }(t,x)\in[0,\widetilde{T}]\times\bar{I}.

Indeed, applying
\partial_{x}
to both sides of (
3.3
),
one gets from (
4.4
) that for
\widetilde{T}:=(1+2Cc_{2})^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\bar{\eta}_{x}(t,x)-1\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\widetilde{T}c_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\ \ \text{for all }(t,x)\in[0,\widetilde{T}]\times\bar{I}.

Finally, it follows from (
3.3
), (
4.4
) and Lemma
A.4
that for all
0\leq t\leq T
,

(4.6)

\begin{gathered}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq C\left\|\bar{\eta}_{xx}(t)\right\|_{1}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\bar{U}_{xx}\|_{1}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{2}t,\\
|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}(t)|_{2}\leq C\sum_{j=3}^{4}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{\eta}(t)|_{2}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j=3}^{4}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}|_{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Cc_{2}t.\end{gathered}

4.1.2.
Proof of Lemma
4.1

The proof will be divided into the following several steps.

Lemma 4.2
.

Under the same assumptions of Lemma
4.1
, it holds that

|\phi_{0}U(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{x}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{t}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t)|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}},

for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+Cc_{2})^{-\frac{6\alpha+2}{\alpha}}\}
.

Proof.

Step 1: Estimate of
\phi_{0}U
.

Multiplying
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, then by (
4.1
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, one gets

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}UU_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{1}}+\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U^{2}-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}U}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}

(4.7)

\displaystyle+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{2}{\alpha}\int\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\big(|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U|_{2}^{2}+|\bar{\eta}_{xx}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U\right|_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U\right|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}+C(c_{2}+c_{2}^{2}t^{2})|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U|_{2}^{2}+C(1+c_{2}^{2}t^{2})c_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}.

For
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, it follows from integration by parts,
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, (
4.1
), (
4.5
), Lemma
A.3
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{1}

\displaystyle=\underline{-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}U^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{\leq 0}-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\big(1+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}.

Thus, combining with (
4.7
)-(
4.8
), one can get from Grönwall’s inequality, (
4.5
) and Lemma
A.5
that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}:=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+Cc_{2})^{-\frac{6\alpha+2}{\alpha}}\}
,

(4.9)

\left|\phi_{0}U(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq Ce^{Cc_{2}^{2}t}\big(\left|\phi_{0}u_{0}\right|_{2}^{2}+c_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}t\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

Step 2: Estimate of
\phi_{0}U_{x}
.
Multiplying
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, then by (
4.1
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{2}}-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.10)

\displaystyle+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{2}{\alpha}\int\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{t}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}+C\big(\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}+Cc_{2}^{2}t^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+C(1+c_{2}^{2}t^{2})c_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}.

For
\mathcal{L}_{2}
, it follows from (
4.1
), (
4.5
), Lemma
A.3
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{x}\big|_{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{t}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})c_{0}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2},

where
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
are arbitrarily small constants that will be determined later.

Hence, it follows from (
4.10
)-(
4.11
) that for all
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
,

(4.12)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})c_{2}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{4+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

For
\phi_{0}U_{xx}
, it follows from (
3.55
), (
4.1
) and (
4.5
)-(
4.6
) that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{1}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+c_{2}t\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)+C\big(c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}+c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}c_{2}t\big),

which, along with Proposition
C.1
, leads to

(4.14)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|\phi_{0}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\big|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+(c_{0}+c_{2}t)\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}(1+c_{2}t)\big).

Substituting (
4.14
) into (
4.12
) and choosing
\varepsilon_{1}
suitably small, one has for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(4.15)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}\leq C(\varepsilon)c_{2}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{4+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

Step 3: Estimates of
\phi_{0}U_{t}
and
\phi_{0}U_{xx}
.

Multiplying
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}
first, applying
U_{t}\partial_{t}
to the resulting equality and finally integrating it over
I
, then according to (
4.1
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tx}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{3}}-\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{xx}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{U}_{xx}U_{x}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.16)

\displaystyle-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{tx}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}+C\big(|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}|\bar{U}_{xx}|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle+C\big(|\bar{U}_{xx}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}+Cc_{2}^{4}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}+c_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}\big)+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}.

For
\mathcal{L}_{3}
, in a similar way as for
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, it follows from integration by parts, (
4.1
), (
4.5
), Lemma
A.3
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

(4.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{3}

\displaystyle=\underline{-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{\leq 0}-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}.

Thus, it follows from (
4.16
)-(
4.17
) that

(4.18)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}\leq Cc_{2}^{4}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}\right|_{2}^{2}+c_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}\big).

Consequently, choosing
\varepsilon
in (
4.15
) sufficiently small, it follows from (
4.15
), (
4.18
), the Grönwall inequality and (
4.5
) that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}
,

(4.19)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}(t)\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Cc_{2}^{4}t}\big(\left|\phi_{0}(u_{0})_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}(0)\right|_{2}^{2}+c_{2}^{6+\frac{2}{\alpha}}t\big)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Cc_{2}^{4}t}\big(c_{0}^{2}+c_{2}^{6+\frac{2}{\alpha}}t\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{2}.

The estimate of
\phi_{0}U_{xx}
can be deduced from (
4.14
) and (
4.19
), that is,

(4.20)

\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}\leq C\big(c_{0}+c_{0}^{2}+c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

Collecting estimates (
4.9
), (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) completes the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Under the same assumptions of Lemma
4.1
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}U_{tx}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U(t)|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U(t)|_{2}\leq Cc_{1}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+Cc_{2})^{-\frac{6\alpha+6}{\alpha}}\}
.

Proof.

Step 1: Estimates of
\phi_{0}U_{tx}
.
Multiplying
\eqref{lp''}_{1}
by
\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}
, applying
U_{tt}\partial_{t}
to the resulting equality and then integrating it over
I
, one can get from (
4.1
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), Lemma
4.2
, Hölder’s inequality, Young’s inequality that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tx}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{tt}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{4}}-2\int\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{U}_{x}U_{t}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{xx}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{U}_{xx}U_{x}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.21)

\displaystyle-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{tx}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{4}+C\big(|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}|\bar{U}_{xx}|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle+C\big(|\bar{U}_{xx}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{4}+Cc_{2}^{2}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{4+\frac{4}{\alpha}}\big)+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}.

For
\mathcal{L}_{4}
, it follows from (
4.1
), (
4.5
), Lemma
A.3
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that for all
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{4}

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}

(4.22)

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})c_{0}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon_{2}|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore, it follows from (
4.21
)-(
4.22
) and (
4.5
) that for all
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|^{2}_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2})c_{2}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|^{2}_{2}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon_{2}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{4+\frac{4}{\alpha}}.

To estimate
\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
, one can take the
L^{2}
-norm of both sides of (
3.60
) and use (
4.1
)-(
4.2
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), and Lemmas
4.2
and
A.4
-
A.5
to conclude that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}
,

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{xx}|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C\left(|\bar{U}_{xx}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}+c_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+c_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}\big),

which, together with Proposition
C.1
, leads to

(4.24)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\big|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}+c_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+c_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}\big).

Hence, plugging (
4.24
) into (
4.23
) and choosing
\varepsilon_{1}
sufficiently small yield that

(4.25)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|^{2}_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}\leq C(\varepsilon)c_{2}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|^{2}_{2}+\varepsilon\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}+Cc_{2}^{4+\frac{4}{\alpha}},

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}
.

Step 2: Estimate of
\phi_{0}U_{tt}
.
Since one has already shown that
U_{tt}
is the weak solution to the problem (
3.50
), then according to Proposition
3.1
,
U_{tt}
satisfies the weak formulation (
3.5
). Thanks to (
3.51
), the test function
\varphi
in (
3.5
) can be replaced by
U_{tt}
; then, according to Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, one deduces from Lemma
A.5
that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{2}U_{tt}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}(\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x})^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\int\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}U_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{5}}+\int\phi_{0}P_{1}^{(2)}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int P_{2}^{(2)}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{5}+C\big(|P_{1}^{(2)}|_{2}^{2}+|P_{2}^{(2)}|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}\big)+\frac{1}{8}|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}|_{2}^{2},

where
(P_{1}^{(2)},P_{2}^{(2)})
is defined as in (
3.49
).

For
\mathcal{L}_{5}
, it follows from (
4.1
), (
4.5
), Lemma
A.3
and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{5}

\displaystyle=\underline{-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int\frac{((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}U^{2}_{tt}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{\leq 0}-\frac{1-2\alpha}{2\alpha}\int\phi_{0}\left(\frac{(\phi_{0})_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}U^{2}_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{4}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}.

For the
L^{2}
-norms of
(P_{1}^{(2)},P_{2}^{(2)})
, it follows from (
4.1
)-(
4.2
), (
4.4
)-(
4.5
), Lemmas
4.2
and
A.4
-
A.5
that

\displaystyle\big|P_{1}^{(2)}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{U}_{tx}\big|_{\infty}\left|U_{x}\right|_{2}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)

(4.28)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{tx}|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{2}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+c_{2}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big)\leq C\big(c_{2}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+c_{2}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}}\big),

\displaystyle\big|P_{2}^{(2)}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{tx}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{U}_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\left|\bar{U}_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)

(4.29)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{U}_{tx}\big|_{\infty}\left|U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{U}_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{2}^{3}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+c_{2}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\big)\leq C\big(c_{2}^{3}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+c_{2}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}}\big).

Thus, it follows from (
4.5
) and (
4.26
)-(
4.29
) that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}|_{2}^{2}\leq C\big(c_{2}^{6}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}^{2}+c_{2}^{6+\frac{4}{\alpha}}\big),

which, along with (
4.5
), (
4.25
) with
\varepsilon
suitably small, and Grönwall inequality, yields that

(4.30)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}(t)\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}(t)\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Cc_{2}^{6}t}\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}(0)\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}(0)\right|_{2}^{2}+c_{2}^{6+\frac{4}{\alpha}}t\big)

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{Cc_{2}^{6}t}\big(c_{0}^{2}+c_{2}^{6+\frac{4}{\alpha}}t\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{2},

for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}:=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+Cc_{2})^{-\frac{6\alpha+6}{\alpha}}\}
.

Step 3: Estimates of
\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U
.
Taking the
L^{2}
-norm of both sides of (
3.63
), one can get from (
4.1
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), (
4.30
), Lemmas
4.2
and
A.5
that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\big(\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{2}\big)

(4.31)

\displaystyle+C\left(\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle+C\big(|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Cc_{0}\left(\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}\right)+Cc_{0}\left(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\right)+C\big(c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}+c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}t^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(c_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}}+c_{0}^{2}+c_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

which, along with Proposition
C.1
and Lemma
4.2
, leads to

(4.32)

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\big|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}\Big)\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

Step 4: Estimate of
\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
.
First, it follows from (
4.30
), (
4.32
), Lemmas
4.2
and
A.4
-
A.5
that for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(4.33)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{1}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{x}^{j}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{1}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}U_{t}(t)|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

\displaystyle\left|U_{x}(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq C\left|U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}\leq C\sum_{j=2}^{3}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}U(t)|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

Next, due to (
3.60
), it follows from (
4.1
)-(
4.2
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), (
4.30
), (
4.33
), Lemmas
4.2
and
A.4
-
A.5
that for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

(4.34)

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}\right|_{2}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{xx}|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C\left(|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{xx}|_{2}\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}+|\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(c_{0}+c_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{1}+c_{2}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}t\big)\leq Cc_{1}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

which, along with Proposition
C.1
and (
4.30
), leads to

(4.35)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\big|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\Big)\leq Cc_{1}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

Step 5: Estimate of
\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U
.
According to (
3.66
), it holds that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+2\left((\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}+2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}\left(\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}+\bar{\eta}_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\right)U_{t}}

\displaystyle\underline{+\left((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{x}+4(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{6}}\underline{-\Big(\frac{2}{\alpha}+1\Big)(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{xx}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}U_{x}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{7}}

\displaystyle\underline{+2\left((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}+2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\right)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{8}}

(4.36)

\displaystyle+\underline{2\phi_{0}\left(\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\right)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{9}}

\displaystyle+\underline{4\left(\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}+(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}-\frac{\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\right)\frac{U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}U}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{10}}

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-1}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-2\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left(\frac{\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\right)}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{11}}

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left(\frac{(\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\right)}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{12}}

\displaystyle+\underline{\frac{2(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{3}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{3}+\frac{6}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}(\phi_{0})_{xx}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{13}}.

It follows from
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, (
4.1
)-(
4.2
), (
4.4
)-(
4.6
), (
4.30
), (
4.32
)-(
4.33
), (
4.35
), Lemmas
4.2
and
A.4
-
A.5
that for all
t\in[0,T_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{6}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|U_{tx}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left(\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\right)\left|U_{t}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}^{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}\big)\left(\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}\right|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C(1+\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3})\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(c_{0}^{2}+c_{2}^{4}t)c_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}}+Cc_{0}c_{1}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}\leq Cc_{1}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{7}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}(\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U|_{2})\leq Cc_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{8}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}\left|U_{x}\right|_{2}+C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}c_{2}^{2}t(|\phi_{0}U_{x}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{xx}|_{2})+Cc_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}t\leq Cc_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}t\leq Cc_{0},

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{9}\right|_{2}
