\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\right|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{3}\big)\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{3}t\leq Cc_{0},

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{10}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|U_{xx}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U|_{2}\leq Cc_{0}^{2+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}t\leq Cc_{0},

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{11}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big(|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big(\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{2}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{3}t\leq Cc_{0},

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{12}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}\big(\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+|\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}c_{2}^{2}t\leq Cc_{0},

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{13}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1-2\alpha}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{3}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}\leq Cc_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}.

Therefore, substituting (
4.37
) into (
4.36
) yields

\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\big|_{2}\leq Cc_{1}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

It follows from Proposition
C.1
and (
4.32
) that

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\big|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}U|_{2}\Big)\leq Cc_{1}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}},

which, along with (
4.30
), (
4.32
) and (
4.35
), completes the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

Now, we can turn back to prove Lemma
4.1
.

Proof.

Collecting the bounds in Lemmas
4.2
-
4.3
, one has

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}},

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{tx}(t)\right|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{3}_{x}U(t)|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}U_{tt}(t)\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\right|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{4}_{x}U(t)|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc_{1}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}}.

Thus, defining the constants
c_{1},c_{2}
and the time
T
as

(4.38)

\displaystyle c_{1}

\displaystyle=Cc_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}},\ \ c_{2}=Cc_{1}^{3+\frac{2}{\alpha}}=C^{4+\frac{2}{\alpha}}c_{0}^{\frac{6\alpha+4}{\alpha^{2}}},\ \ T=\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\}\leq(1+Cc_{2})^{-\frac{6\alpha+6}{\alpha}},

one can arrive at the uniform estimates (
4.3
).
The proof of Lemma
4.1
is completed.
∎

4.2.
Uniform estimates for the case
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1

Analogous to Lemma
4.1
, one can establish the following uniform bounds.

Lemma 4.4
.

Consider (
3.2
). Assume that
U
is the unique classical solution in
[0,T]\times\bar{I}
to the problem (
3.1
) obtained in Lemma
3.1
, and the positive constant
c_{0}
satisfies

(4.39)

2+\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}+\widetilde{E}(0,U)\leq c_{0}.

Then there exist a positive time
T_{*}\in(0,T]
and constants
c_{i}
,
i=1,2
,
1<c_{0}\leq c_{1}\leq c_{2}
, which depend only on
c_{0}
,
\alpha
,
\varepsilon_{0}
,
|I|
,
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, such that if for all
0\leq t\leq T_{*}
,

(4.40)

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\bar{U}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\bar{U}_{t}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}\leq c_{1},

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\bar{U}_{tx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial^{3}_{x}\bar{U}(t)\big|_{2}\leq c_{2},

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\bar{U}_{tt}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}\bar{U}_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial^{4}_{x}\bar{U}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}\leq c_{2},

it holds that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{*}
,

(4.41)

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{x}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}\leq c_{1},

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial^{3}_{x}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq c_{2},

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial^{4}_{x}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq c_{2}.

Proof.

We only sketch the proof here. Denote by
\mathscr{P}(\cdot)
the generic polynomial functions with the form
\mathscr{P}(s)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}p_{j}s^{j}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
0\leq p_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
.

Step 1: Some useful estimates.
First, it is convenient to give some useful estimates as in (
4.4
)-(
4.6
). According to Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
, one has that for all
0\leq t\leq T
,

(4.42)

\begin{split}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{x}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}(t)\big|_{2}\leq&\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),\\
\|\bar{U}_{x}(t)\|_{1,\infty}\leq C\|\bar{U}_{x}(t)\|_{2,1}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}(t)\big|_{2}\leq&\mathscr{P}(c_{2}),\\
|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{U}(t)|_{\infty}\leq C\sum_{j=3}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{U}(t)\big|_{2}\leq&\mathscr{P}(c_{2}).\end{split}

Next, one can find a small constant
\widetilde{T}:=(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2}))^{-1}
such that

(4.43)

\frac{1}{2}\leq\bar{\eta}_{x}(t,x)\leq\frac{3}{2},\quad\text{for all }(t,x)\in[0,\widetilde{T}]\times\bar{I}.

Finally, it holds that for all
0\leq t\leq T
,

(4.44)

\begin{split}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq C\left\|\bar{\eta}_{xx}(t)\right\|_{1,1}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\bar{U}_{xx}\|_{1,1}\leq&\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t,\\
\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C\sum_{j=2}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq&\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t.\end{split}

Step 2: Analogy of Lemma
4.2
.

First, following the proof in
Step 1
of Lemma
4.2
, one obtains that

(4.45)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}:=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2}))^{-M}\}
, for some large
M>0
.

Next, based on the proof in
Step 2
of Lemma
4.2
, multiplying (
3.2
) by
U_{t}
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, one has

(4.46)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{x}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{2}{\alpha}\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{t}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Note that, comparing (
4.46
) with (
4.10
), there is no crossing term
\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\right)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}U_{t}\,\mathrm{d}x

in (
4.46
). Thus, according to (
4.42
)-(
4.43
), Lemma
A.5
, Hölder’s inequality, Young’s inequality and Grönwall’s inequality, one has

(4.47)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{x}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

Similarly, applying
U_{t}\partial_{t}
to both sides of (
3.2
) and integrating the resulting equality over
I
yield

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{t}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x=\int\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x,

which, by the analogous calculations, along with (
4.45
) and (
4.47
), implies that

(4.48)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

To get the estimate of
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}
, recall (
3.79
),

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}.

One can take the
L^{2}
-norm of the above equality to get that

(4.49)

\displaystyle\Big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\Big|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\big|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\big|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{x}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\left(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\right)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}).

Next, it follows from the arguments for (
3.47
) and the estimate (
4.48
) that

\displaystyle\left|U_{x}(t,x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}+C\phi_{0}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\big),

which, along with
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
, implies that for all
\iota>0
,

(4.50)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\iota}U_{x}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(\iota)\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

Hence, if
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}
, one can set
\iota=\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}
in (
4.50
), and get from (
4.49
) that

(4.51)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}(t)\big|\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{1};

while, if
\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}
, (
4.49
) can be reduced to

(4.52)

\displaystyle\Big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}\Big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

and then one can deduce from Proposition
C.1
and (
4.47
) that

(4.53)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{1}.

In conclusion, it holds that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{1}
,

(4.54)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{x}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}).

Step 3: Analogy of Lemma
4.3
.

Step 3.1: Tangential estimates.
As in
Steps 1
-
2
of the proof for Lemma
4.3
, one can apply
U_{tt}\partial_{t}
to both sides of (
3.2
) to get the 3rd order estimates, and use the weak formulation for the equation of
U_{tt}
to get the 4th order estimates, that is,

(4.55)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tt}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}U_{tx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int\bigg(\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\bigg)_{x}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\bigg(\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\bigg)_{x}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x,

and

(4.56)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{tt}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x})^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\frac{6\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{4}}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{tx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int\frac{4\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}U_{tx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-\int\frac{6\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\bar{U}_{x}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{4}}\partial_{t}^{2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Again, different from (
4.21
), there is no crossing term

\left(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\right)\int\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}U_{tt}\,\mathrm{d}x

in (
4.55
). Hence, it follows from similar computations as in
Step 1
and
Step 2
of Lemma
4.3
that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}:=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2}))^{-M^{\prime}}\}
, for some large
M^{\prime}>0
,

(4.57)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}).

Step 3.2: Estimate of
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U
.

According to (
3.87
), one has

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{x}+\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{t}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{14}}

(4.58)

\displaystyle\underline{+\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}U_{t}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{15}}

\displaystyle\underline{+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{16}}

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{17}}.

Taking the
L^{2}
-norm of both sides of the above equality, one gets from (
1.18
), (
4.42
)-(
4.44
), (
4.54
), (
4.57
) and Lemma
A.5
that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{14}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{x}\big|_{2}+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}U\big|_{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\partial_{x}^{j}U_{t}\big|_{2}\bigg)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{15}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{x}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}U\big|_{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\partial_{x}^{j}U_{t}\big|_{2}\bigg)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

(4.59)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{16}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{x}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{x}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}U\big|_{2}+1\bigg)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{17}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\big|_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big|_{2}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}).

Then, it follows from (
4.58
)-(
4.59
) that

\Big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{xx}\Big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

which, together with (
4.54
) and Proposition
C.1
, leads to

(4.60)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{2}.

Step 3.3: Estimate of
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}
.

First, according to
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
, (
4.54
), (
4.57
), (
4.60
) and Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
, one gets that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

(4.61)

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}(t)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{1}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}\partial_{x}^{j}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}(t)\big|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\varepsilon_{0}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{tx}(t)\big|_{2}\big)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

\displaystyle\left|U_{x}(t)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|U_{x}(t)\right\|_{1,1}\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{3}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}).

Then it follows from (
3.84
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
)-(
4.44
), (
4.54
), (
4.57
), (
4.61
) and Lemma
A.5
that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\Big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}+C\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{x}\big|_{2}\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{\infty}+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{xx}\big|_{2}

(4.62)

\displaystyle+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{x}\big|_{2}+C\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{xx}\big|_{2}\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{U}_{x}\big|_{2}\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}(c_{0})(\mathscr{P}(c_{1})+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}).

Next, as for (
3.82
), one gets from (
4.48
), (
4.57
) and Lemma
A.5
that

|U_{tx}(t,x)|\leq C\phi_{0}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}|\phi_{0}\bar{U}_{x}|_{\infty}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}\big)+C\phi_{0}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1})\phi_{0}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2\alpha}},

which, along with
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
, implies that for all
\iota>0
,

(4.63)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1+\iota}U_{tx}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(\iota)\mathscr{P}(c_{1})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{2}.

Hence, if
0<\varepsilon_{0}<\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}
, one can set
\iota=\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}
in (
4.63
), and get from (
4.62
) that

(4.64)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\big|\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{2};

while, if
\varepsilon_{0}=\frac{3\alpha-1}{2\alpha}
, (
4.62
) can be reduced to

(4.65)

\displaystyle\Big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{tx}\Big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

and then one can deduce from Proposition
C.1
and (
4.57
) that

(4.66)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{2}.

Step 3.4: Estimate of
\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U
.

According to (
3.92
), one has

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}\partial_{t}U_{xx}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left((\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{x}+2\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{tx}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left(\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}+\bar{\eta}_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\right)U_{t}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{18}}

\displaystyle\underline{+\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{x}+4(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right)\bar{\eta}_{x}U_{t}-\Big(\frac{2}{\alpha}+1\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{xx}U_{xx}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{19}}

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{1}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}U_{x}+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big((\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}+2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\Big)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{20}}

(4.67)

\displaystyle\underline{+2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\Big)\frac{U_{x}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{21}}

\displaystyle\underline{+4\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}+(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}-\frac{\phi_{0}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}\Big)\frac{U_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}U}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{22}}

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha^{2}}\frac{\phi_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-2\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{3\bar{\eta}_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}+\frac{2\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{3}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{3}}\Big)}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{23}}

\displaystyle\underline{-\frac{2+2\alpha}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\varepsilon_{0}}\Big(\frac{(\phi_{0})_{xx}\bar{\eta}_{xx}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}+\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}}-\frac{2(\phi_{0})_{x}\bar{\eta}_{xx}^{2}}{\bar{\eta}_{x}^{2}}\Big)}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{24}}

\displaystyle\underline{+\frac{2(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{3}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{3}+\frac{6}{\alpha^{2}}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}(\phi_{0})_{xx}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}}_{:=\mathcal{L}_{25}}.

It follows from (
1.18
), (
4.42
)-(
4.44
), (
4.54
), (
4.57
), (
4.60
)-(
4.61
), (
4.64
) and Lemma
A.5
that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{18}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}\big|_{2}+C\left(\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\right)\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{tx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big(\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}\Big)\left|\phi_{0}U_{t}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1})+\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\left(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\right)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{19}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\right)\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{t}\big|_{2}+C\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\left(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\right)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{20}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}\right|_{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{x}\big|_{\infty}+C\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}\big(\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{x}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\left(1+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\right)\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

(4.68)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{21}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\big|_{2}\Big)\left|U_{x}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{22}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}|_{\infty}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big)\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}U_{xx}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}U\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{23}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big|_{2}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\Big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{24}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}\left\|\phi_{0}\right\|_{3}\Big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}\big|_{2}+\left|\bar{\eta}_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\bar{\eta}_{xx}\big|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathscr{P}(c_{0})\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t\leq C\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}_{25}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\underline{\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big|_{2}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{3}}_{(=0,\text{ if }\alpha=1)}+C\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\big|_{2}\left|(\phi_{0})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad+C\left|\phi_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{3}\phi_{0}|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}).

Then, it follows from (
4.67
)-(
4.68
) that

\Big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}+2\Big)\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}(\phi_{0})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}U\Big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{0}),

which, together with (
4.60
) and Proposition
C.1
, implies that

(4.69)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{4}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1})\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T_{2}.

Collecting all estimates (
4.57
), (
4.60
), (
4.64
) and (
4.69
) yields that for all
0\leq t\leq T_{2}
,

(4.70)

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial^{3}_{x}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}),

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}U_{tt}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{t}U_{xx}(t)\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial^{4}_{x}U(t)\big|_{2}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{1}).

Step 4: Choices of
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
T
.
Defining the constants
c_{1},c_{2}
and the time
T
as

(4.71)

c_{1}=\sqrt{\mathscr{P}(c_{0})},\quad c_{2}=\sqrt{\mathscr{P}(c_{1})},\quad T=\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\},

according to (
4.54
) and (
4.70
), one then obtain the uniform estimates (
4.41
).

The proof of Lemma
4.4
is completed.
∎

5.
Local existence and uniqueness for the nonlinear problem

§
5
is devoted to proving the local existence of the unique classical solution for problem (
2.1
)-(
2.2
), i.e., Theorem
1.1
, and Theorem
1.3
follows as a consequence. For simplicity, we only prove the case of
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, and the case of
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
can be treated analogously.

5.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

This will be divided into the following several steps:

Step 1: Construction of the iterative sequence.

We use the same notations as in Lemma
4.1
. Set

U^{0}(t,x):=u_{0},\quad\eta^{0}(t,x)=x+tu_{0}.

Then, for given
c_{0}
as in Lemma
4.1
and
c_{i}
,
i=1,2
, defined by (
4.38
), there exists a small positive time
T^{\prime}\leq T:=\min\{\widetilde{T},(1+Cc_{2})^{-\frac{6\alpha+6}{\alpha}}\}
, such that for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
,

(5.1)

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}U^{0}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{x}^{0}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{t}^{0}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{xx}^{0}(t)|_{2}\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{x}^{0}(t)-1|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}U^{0}_{tx}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{3}_{x}U^{0}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{tt}^{0}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}^{0}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{4}_{x}U^{0}(t)|_{2}\leq c_{2}.

Next, let
(\bar{U},\bar{\eta})=(U^{0},\eta^{0})
in (
3.1
) be the first generation of solutions. According to Lemma
3.1
, there exists a unique classical solution
(U^{1},\eta^{1})
to the problem (
3.1
). Clearly, one can deduce from Lemma
4.3
that
U^{1}
satisfies (
5.1
), which implies that
|\eta_{x}^{1}-1|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2}
on
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
for the same
T^{\prime}
.

Then, the approximate sequence
(U^{k+1},\eta^{k+1})
,
k\geq 1
, can be constructed as follows: given
(U^{k},\eta^{k})
, define
(U^{k+1},\eta^{k+1})
by solving the following problem,

(5.2)

\begin{cases}\quad\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t}^{k+1}-\left(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}^{k+1}}{(\eta^{k}_{x})^{2}}\right)_{x}\\
\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}^{k+1}}{(\eta^{k}_{x})^{2}}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}}{(\eta^{k}_{x})^{2}}\bigg)_{x}+\Big(2-\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}}{(\eta^{k}_{x})^{2}}&\text{in }(0,T]\times I,\\
\quad\eta^{k+1}_{t}=U^{k+1}&\text{in }(0,T]\times I,\\[4.0pt]
\quad(U^{k+1},\eta^{k+1})=(u_{0},\mathrm{id})&\text{on }\{t=0\}\times I.\end{cases}

It follows from Lemma
4.1
that one can successfully obtain an iterative solution sequence
(U^{k},\eta^{k})
satisfying (
5.1
), that is, for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
and all
k\geq 0
,

(5.3)

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}U^{k}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{x}^{k}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{t}^{k}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{xx}^{k}(t)|_{2}\leq c_{1},\quad|\eta_{x}^{k}(t)-1|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{2},

\displaystyle|\phi_{0}U^{k}_{tx}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{3}_{x}U^{k}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}U_{tt}^{k}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{t}U_{xx}^{k}(t)|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial^{4}_{x}U^{k}(t)|_{2}\leq c_{2}.

Step 2: Convergence of
(U^{k},\eta^{k})
.

Set

(5.4)

\widehat{U}^{k+1}:=U^{k+1}-U^{k},\quad\widehat{\eta}^{k+1}:=\eta^{k+1}-\eta^{k}=\int_{0}^{t}\widehat{U}^{k+1}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s,

and introduce the following basic energy function:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}^{k}|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}^{k}_{x}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

It follows from (
5.2
) that

(5.5)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{2}\widehat{U}_{t}^{k+1}\!\!-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k+1}}{(\eta_{x}^{k})^{2}}\bigg)_{x}\!\!\!=\!\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k+1}}{(\eta_{x}^{k})^{2}}+(\phi_{0}\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k})_{x}+\mathcal{R}_{2}^{k}&\text{in }(0,T^{\prime}]\times I,\\
\widehat{\eta}^{k+1}_{t}=\widehat{U}^{k+1},&\text{in }(0,T^{\prime}]\times I,\\[4.0pt]
(\widehat{U}^{k+1},\widehat{\eta}^{k+1})=(0,0)&\text{on }\{t=0\}\times I,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}=\big(\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}-\phi_{0}U_{x}^{k}\big)\frac{(\eta_{x}^{k}+\eta_{x}^{k-1})\widehat{\eta}_{x}^{k}}{(\eta_{x}^{k}\eta_{x}^{k-1})^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2}^{k}=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}-U_{x}^{k}\big)\frac{(\eta_{x}^{k}+\eta_{x}^{k-1})\widehat{\eta}_{x}^{k}}{(\eta_{x}^{k}\eta_{x}^{k-1})^{2}}.

Then, one can conclude from (
5.3
)-(
5.4
) and
\widehat{\eta}_{tx}^{k}=\widehat{U}^{k}_{x}
that

(5.6)

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}(t)|_{2}^{2}+|\mathcal{R}_{2}^{k}(t)|_{2}^{2}\leq Ct\int_{0}^{t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Next, multiplying
\eqref{398}_{1}
by
\widehat{U}^{k+1}
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, then following the proofs of (
4.7
)-(
4.8
), one can get from (
5.6
), Lemma
A.5
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}^{k+1}|_{2}^{2}+|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k+1}|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}^{k+1}|_{2}^{2}+|\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}(t)|_{2}^{2}+|\mathcal{R}_{2}^{k}(t)|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}^{k+1}|_{2}^{2}+Ct\int_{0}^{t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, along with Grönwall’s inequality, leads to

(5.7)

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(t)\leq Ct^{2}e^{Ct}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}\text{ and all }k\geq 1.

Choosing
t=T_{*}
such that
CT_{*}^{2}e^{CT_{*}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
T_{*}\leq T^{\prime}
, then one can get

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(T_{*})\leq\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(T_{*})\ \ \text{for all }k\geq 1,

which yields

(5.8)

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(T_{*})\leq\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\bigg)\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{1}(T_{*})\leq C(c_{0}).

Then, it follows from (
5.8
) that

\displaystyle\phi_{0}U^{m}-\phi_{0}U^{n}\to 0\quad\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}U^{m}_{x}-\phi_{0}U^{n}_{x}\to 0\quad\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),

as
m,n\to\infty
, which implies that
\{U^{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
converges to a unique limit
U
as
k\to\infty
in the following sense:

(5.9)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}U^{k}\to\phi_{0}U\quad\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];L^{2}),\quad\phi_{0}U^{k}_{x}\to\phi_{0}U_{x}\quad\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];L^{2}).

This, together with (
5.3
), (
5.5
), Lemmas
A.2
-
A.3
and
A.5
, shows that

(5.10)

\displaystyle U^{k}\to U

\displaystyle\text{in }C([0,T_{*}];H^{s_{1}}),\quad\text{for all }s_{1}\in[0,3),

\displaystyle U_{t}^{k}\to U_{t}

\displaystyle\text{in }L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{s_{2}}),\ \ \text{for all }s_{2}\in[0,1).

Since
\{U^{k}\}_{k\geq 0}\subset C([0,T_{*}];H^{3})
by Lemma
3.1
, one gets from
(
5.10
)
that
U\in C([0,T_{*}];H^{s_{1}})
,
U_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T_{*}];H^{s_{2}})
. Then, letting
k\to\infty
in
\eqref{396}_{1}
-
\eqref{396}_{2}
, one gets that
\eqref{fp''}_{1}
holds for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T_{*})\times I
and
\eqref{fp''}_{2}
holds continuously.

Next, letting
k\to\infty
in
\eqref{396}_{1}
, one deduces from
\eqref{fp''}_{1}
and (
5.10
) that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t}^{k}\to

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}+\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2+\frac{1}{\alpha}}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}

\displaystyle+\Big(2-\frac{1}{\alpha}\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}(\phi_{0})_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\quad\text{in }C([0,T]\times\bar{I}).

This, along with
\eqref{3126}_{2}
and the uniqueness of the limits, implies that

\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t}^{k}\to\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t}\quad\text{in }C([0,T]\times\bar{I}),\quad\phi_{0}^{2}U_{t}\in C([0,T]\times\bar{I}).

Hence,
\eqref{fp''}_{1}
holds continuously.

Moreover, it follows from the lower semi-continuity of weak convergence that (
5.3
) still holds for
U
, that is,
\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}E(t,U)<\infty
, which, along with Lemma
A.5
, leads to

(5.12)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}|\partial_{x}^{j}U|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\left(|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j}U|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{j+1}U|_{2}\right)\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}E(t,U)<\infty;

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}|\partial_{x}^{k}U_{t}|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\big(|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{k}U_{t}|_{2}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{k+1}U_{t}|_{2}\big)\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}E(t,U)<\infty,

for
j=0,1,2,3
and
k=0,1
, that is,
U\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{3})\cap W^{1,\infty}([0,T_{*}];H^{1})
.

The proof of the existence is completed.

Step 3: Uniqueness, time continuity and (
1.23
).

Suppose that there exist two solutions
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
on
[0,T_{*}]\times\bar{I}
. Define

\begin{split}\eta_{i}(t,x):=&x+\int_{0}^{t}U_{i}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s,\quad\widehat{\eta}:=\eta_{2}-\eta_{1},\quad\widehat{U}:=U_{2}-U_{1},\\
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}(t):=&\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}|_{2}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Then, by (
2.1
),
(\widehat{U},\widehat{\eta})
solves the following problem

(5.13)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{2}\widehat{U}_{t}-\bigg(\frac{\phi_{0}^{2}\widehat{U}_{x}}{(\eta_{2})_{x}^{2}}\bigg)_{x}=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\frac{\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\widehat{U}_{x}}{(\eta_{2})_{x}^{2}}+(\phi_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{1})_{x}+\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{2}&\text{in }(0,T^{\prime}]\times I,\\
\widehat{\eta}_{t}=\widehat{U}&\text{in }(0,T^{\prime}]\times I,\\[4.0pt]
(\widehat{U},\widehat{\eta})=(0,0)&\text{on }\{t=0\}\times I,\end{cases}

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{1}=\big(\phi_{0}^{1+\frac{1}{\alpha}}-\phi_{0}(U_{1})_{x}\big)\frac{((\eta_{1})_{x}+(\eta_{2})_{x})\widehat{\eta}_{x}}{((\eta_{1})_{x}(\eta_{2})_{x})^{2}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{2}=\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}-2\Big)\phi_{0}(\phi_{0})_{x}\big(\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}-(U_{1})_{x}\big)\frac{((\eta_{1})_{x}+(\eta_{2})_{x})\widehat{\eta}_{x}}{((\eta_{1})_{x}(\eta_{2})_{x})^{2}}.

Clearly,
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{i}

(i=1,2)
satisfies (
5.6
) with
\widehat{U}^{k}
replaced by
\widehat{U}
, that is,

(5.14)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{1}(t)|_{2}^{2}+|\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{2}(t)|_{2}^{2}\leq Ct\int_{0}^{t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Hence, following the proof of
Step 2
, multiplying
\eqref{fp'}_{1}
by
\widehat{U}
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, one can get from (
5.14
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}|_{2}^{2}+|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}|_{2}^{2}\leq C|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}|_{2}^{2}+Ct\int_{0}^{t}|\phi_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s,

which, along with Grönwall’s inequality and the definition of
T_{*}
, leads to
\widehat{\mathcal{E}}(t)\equiv 0
for all
0\leq t\leq T_{*}
.
Therefore, it follows from Lemma
A.5
that
U_{1}\equiv U_{2}
.

The time continuity can be shown by following the proofs in Lemma
3.1
, which is omitted here for simplicity. Finally, (
1.23
) is a direct consequence of Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
. Therefore, one completes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Remark 5.1
.

For the case
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
, the corresponding estimates in (
5.12
) become

(5.15)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}|\partial_{x}^{j}U|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}U\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j+1}U\big|_{2}\big)\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\widetilde{E}(t,U);

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}|\partial_{x}^{k}U_{t}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\big(\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{k}U_{t}\big|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{k+1}U_{t}\big|_{2}\big)\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T_{*}]}\widetilde{E}(t,U),

for
j=0,1,2,3
and
k=0,1
, that is,
U\in L^{\infty}([0,T_{*}];W^{3,1})\cap W^{1,\infty}([0,T_{*}];W^{1,1})
.

5.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Define
(\rho(t,y),u(t,y))
by (
E.1
) in Appendix
E
. Then it follows from Theorem
1.1
and (
E.3
) that
(\rho(t,y),u(t,y),\Gamma(t))
becomes the unique classical solution in
\overline{\mathbb{I}(T_{*})}
to the
VFBP
(
1.7
) satisfying (
1.28
) or (
1.29
), which completes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

5.3.
Local existence and uniqueness with a general initial flow map

To carry out the continuation argument in §
8
, we allow a general initial flow map. More precisely, let
\tau\geq 0
be fixed, and let
u_{*}=u_{*}(x)
and
\eta_{*}=\eta_{*}(x)
be given functions. Consider a flow map
\eta=\eta(t,x)
satisfying

(5.16)

\eta(t,x)=\eta_{*}(x)+\int_{\tau}^{t}U(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s\quad\text{for $t\geq\tau$},

where
\eta_{*}
satisfies the following regularity properties:

(5.17)

c_{*}\leq(\eta_{*})_{x}\leq c^{*}\text{ with $0<c_{*}\leq c^{*}<\infty$},\quad\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\eta_{*})_{x}\big|_{2}+\sum_{k=2}^{4}\big|\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\alpha}\partial_{x}^{k}\eta_{*}\big|_{2}<\infty.

Retaining the original Lagrangian labels, the conserved reference density remains
\rho_{0}=H\eta_{x}
. For
T>0
, we therefore consider the following version of (
1.16
) on
(\tau,\tau+T]\times I
:

(5.18)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\rho_{0}U_{t}+\left(\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}-\left(\frac{\rho_{0}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}=0&\text{in}\ \ (\tau,\tau+T]\times I,\\[2.0pt]
\displaystyle\eta_{t}=U&\text{in}\ \ (\tau,\tau+T]\times I,\\[2.0pt]
(U,\eta)=(u_{*},\eta_{*})&\text{on}\ \ \{t=\tau\}\times I.\end{cases}

We aim to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let (
1.8
) hold for
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
. Assume
(\rho_{0},u_{*},\eta_{*})
satisfies (
5.17
) and

(5.19)

2+\|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\|_{3}+\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\eta_{*})_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{k=2}^{4}\big|\rho_{0}^{(\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0})\alpha}\partial_{x}^{k}\eta_{*}\big|_{2}^{2}+\widetilde{E}(\tau,U)\leq\mathfrak{M}\quad\text{for some $\mathfrak{M}>0$},

then there exists a time
T_{*}>0
, which depends only on
(c_{*},c^{*},\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\alpha,\varepsilon_{0},|I|,\mathfrak{M})
and is independent of
\tau
, such that problem (
5.18
) with
T=T_{*}
admits a unique classical solution
(U,\eta)
in
[\tau,\tau+T_{*}]\times\bar{I}
satisfying

(5.20)

\begin{gathered}U\in\mathscr{C}([\tau,\tau+T_{*}];\widetilde{E}),\quad\eta\in\mathscr{C}^{1}([\tau,\tau+T_{*}];\widetilde{E});\\
\frac{c_{*}}{2}\leq\eta_{x}(t,x)\leq 2c^{*}\ \ \text{for all }(t,x)\in[\tau,\tau+T_{*}]\times\bar{I}.\end{gathered}

Moreover, (
1.22
)-(
1.23
) hold on
(\tau,\tau+T_{*}]
and (
1.26
) holds on
[\tau,\tau+T_{*}]\times I
.

Remark 5.2
.

We note that problem (
5.18
) is used only for the continuation argument in §
8
and is not equivalent to (
1.14
). The temporal derivatives at
t=\tau
entering
\widetilde{E}(\tau,U)
are determined recursively from (
5.18
), which are similar to (
B.1
) in Appendix
B
:

(5.21)

\displaystyle U_{t}(\tau,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{0}(u_{*})_{x}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}-\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}\Big)_{x},

\displaystyle U_{tx}(\tau,x)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{\rho_{0}(u_{*})_{x}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}-\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}\big)_{x}\Big)_{x},

\displaystyle U_{txx}(\tau,x)

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\big(\frac{\rho_{0}(u_{*})_{x}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}-\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}\big)_{x}\Big)_{xx},

\displaystyle U_{tt}(\tau,x)

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}^{-1}\Big(\frac{\rho_{0}U_{tx}(\tau,x)}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}-2\frac{\rho_{0}(u_{*})_{x}^{2}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{3}}+2\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}(u_{*})_{x}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{3}}\Big)_{x}.

Following the same argument as in Appendix
B
, we can show that (
5.21
) implies the compatibility condition
(u_{*})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
. Indeed,
\eqref{1..16z}_{1}
implies

(5.22)

U_{t}(\tau,x)=\frac{1}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{2}}\Big((u_{*})_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}}{\rho_{0}^{\alpha}}(u_{*})_{x}-\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\Big)-\frac{2\big((u_{*})_{x}-\rho_{0}\big)(\eta_{*})_{xx}}{(\eta_{*})_{x}^{3}}.

Then, taking the
L^{\infty}
norm, (
1.8
), (
5.19
), and Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
give

\displaystyle|\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(u_{*})_{x}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|U_{t}(\tau)\right|_{\infty}+\left\|(u_{*})_{x}\right\|_{1,\infty}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\|_{1,\infty}\big)\big(1+|(\eta_{*})_{xx}|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\widetilde{E}(\tau,U)+C\leq C,

where
C
depends only on
(c_{*},c^{*},\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\alpha,\mathfrak{M})
. Since
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
, we obtain
(u_{*})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

We adapt the construction in §
3
-§
5
. Throughout the proof,
C
denotes a
generic positive constant, and
\mathscr{P}
denotes a generic polynomial
with positive coefficients. Both
C
and the coefficients of

\mathscr{P}
depend only on

(c_{*},c^{*},\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\alpha,\varepsilon_{0},|I|,\mathfrak{M})
and are independent of
\tau
. Recall that

\phi_{0}=\rho_{0}^{\alpha}
. The bound in (
5.19
) can be written as

(5.23)

2+\|\phi_{0}\|_{3}+\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}(\eta_{*})_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{k=2}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{k}\eta_{*}\big|_{2}^{2}+\widetilde{E}(\tau,U)\leq\mathfrak{M}.

Step 1: Estimates for a general initial flow map.

The temporal derivatives at
t=\tau
are defined recursively by (
5.21
).
Thus, (
5.19
) controls all the initial terms occurring in the proof of
Lemma
4.4
. In particular, the compatibility
calculation following (
5.22
) gives

|(u_{*})_{x}(x)|\leq C\rho_{0}^{\alpha}(x)\leq Cd(x)\quad\text{for all }x\in\bar{I},\qquad(u_{*})_{x}\big|_{\Gamma}=0.

We first derive the coefficient estimates that replace
(
4.43
)–(
4.44
). Since

0<\varepsilon_{0}<1
by (
1.18
), (
5.23
), together with
Lemmas
A.4
–
A.5
,
implies

(5.24)

|(\eta_{*})_{xx}|_{\infty}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta_{*}|_{\infty}+\sum_{j=2}^{3}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\eta_{*}\big|_{2}+\sum_{j=2}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\eta_{*}\big|_{2}\leq C.

Let
\bar{U}
satisfy the bounds in (
4.40
), with
[0,T]

replaced by
[\tau,\tau+T]
, and set

(5.25)

\bar{\eta}(t,x)=\eta_{*}(x)+\int_{\tau}^{t}\bar{U}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s.

The argument for (
4.42
) gives

\|\bar{U}_{x}(t)\|_{1,\infty}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{U}(t)|_{\infty}\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{2}).

Combining (
5.24
)–(
5.25
),
we obtain

(5.26)

\displaystyle|\bar{\eta}_{x}(t)-(\eta_{*})_{x}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\mathscr{P}(c_{2})(t-\tau),

\displaystyle|\bar{\eta}_{xx}(t)|_{\infty}+|\phi_{0}\partial_{x}^{3}\bar{\eta}(t)|_{\infty}+\sum_{j=2}^{3}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{\eta}(t)\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})(t-\tau),

\displaystyle\sum_{j=2}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\bar{\eta}(t)\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})(t-\tau).

We choose
T>0
sufficiently small so that

\mathscr{P}(c_{2})T\leq\frac{c_{*}}{2}.

It then follows from (
5.26
) and
c_{*}\leq(\eta_{*})_{x}\leq c^{*}
that

(5.27)

\frac{c_{*}}{2}\leq\bar{\eta}_{x}(t,x)\leq c^{*}+\frac{c_{*}}{2}\leq 2c^{*}\quad\text{for all }(t,x)\in[\tau,\tau+T]\times\bar{I}.

Thus, (
5.26
)–(
5.27
)
are precisely the general-initial-map counterparts of
(
4.43
)–(
4.44
).

Step 2: Construction and uniform estimates.

Take

U^{0}(t,x)=u_{*}(x),\qquad\eta^{0}(t,x)=\eta_{*}(x)+(t-\tau)u_{*}(x).

For
k\geq 0
, once
(U^{k},\eta^{k})
is known, we define

(U^{k+1},\eta^{k+1})
by

(5.28)

\begin{cases}\displaystyle\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{t}^{k+1}-\Big(\dfrac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}U_{x}^{k+1}}{(\eta_{x}^{k})^{2}}\Big)_{x}+\Big(\dfrac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}}{(\eta_{x}^{k})^{2}}\Big)_{x}=0&\text{in }(\tau,\tau+T]\times I,\\
\displaystyle\eta^{k+1}(t,x)=\eta_{*}(x)+\int_{\tau}^{t}U^{k+1}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s&\text{in }[\tau,\tau+T]\times I,\\[4.0pt]
(U^{k+1},\eta^{k+1})=(u_{*},\eta_{*})&\text{on }\{t=\tau\}\times I.\end{cases}

First, we find that the proof of Lemma
3.1
ii), based on Proposition
3.2
, applies to
\eqref{general-flow-iteration}_{1}
. Indeed, the Galerkin argument uses only the positive lower and upper bounds for
\eta_{x}^{k}
and the weighted bounds for its spatial derivatives, rather than the identity
\eta^{k}(\tau,x)=x
. These coefficient bounds follow from
(
5.26
)–(
5.27
).
The initial values of
U_{t}^{k+1}
,
U_{tx}^{k+1}
,

U_{txx}^{k+1}
, and
U_{tt}^{k+1}
are the quantities determined by
the recursive formulas in (
5.21
).

We next apply the proof of Lemma
4.4
. The identities (
3.79
), (
3.84
), (
3.87
), and
(
3.92
) remain unchanged, since
\bar{\eta}_{t}=\bar{U}
. In the estimates following
(
4.49
), (
4.58
), (
4.62
), and (
4.67
),
we use (
5.26
) in place of
(
4.44
). Hence, every factor

\mathscr{P}(c_{2})t
arising from a higher-order derivative of the flow map
is replaced by

C+\mathscr{P}(c_{2})(t-\tau).

All the time integrals are now taken over
[\tau,t]
, so every small factor
t
in the original proof is replaced by
t-\tau
. The additional constant is controlled by (
5.23
) and can be incorporated into
\mathscr{P}(c_{0})
. Therefore, the arguments leading to (
4.54
), (
4.57
), (
4.60
), (
4.64
), and (
4.69
)–(
4.70
) remain valid, with constants depending only on
(c_{*},c^{*},\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\alpha,\varepsilon_{0},|I|,\mathfrak{M})
.

Taking
c_{0}=\mathfrak{M}
and choosing
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
, and
T
successively
as in
Step 4
of the proof of Lemma
4.4
, we
obtain, after decreasing
T
if necessary,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\frac{c_{*}}{2}\leq\eta_{x}^{k}(t,x)\leq 2c^{*}\quad\text{for all }(t,x)\in[\tau,\tau+T]\times\bar{I},

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 0}\sup_{t\in[\tau,\tau+T]}\widetilde{E}(t,U^{k})\leq C,\qquad\sup_{k\geq 0}\sup_{t\in[\tau,\tau+T]}\sum_{j=2}^{4}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon_{0}}\partial_{x}^{j}\eta^{k}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C.

In particular,
T
depends only on

(c_{*},c^{*},\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},\alpha,\varepsilon_{0},|I|,\mathfrak{M})
and is independent of
\tau
.

Step 3: Convergence of the approximate solutions.

Set

\widehat{U}^{k+1}=U^{k+1}-U^{k},\qquad\widehat{\eta}^{k+1}=\eta^{k+1}-\eta^{k}=\int_{\tau}^{t}\widehat{U}^{k+1}(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s,

and define

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)=\sup_{s\in[\tau,t]}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{k}(s)\big|_{2}^{2}+\int_{\tau}^{t}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k}(s)\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

For
k\geq 1
, subtracting two consecutive equations in
(
5.28
) gives

(5.30)

\rho_{0}\widehat{U}_{t}^{k+1}-\Big(\frac{\rho_{0}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k+1}}{(\eta_{x}^{k})^{2}}\Big)_{x}=\big(\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}\big)_{x},

where

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}=\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho_{0}-U_{x}^{k})\frac{(\eta_{x}^{k}+\eta_{x}^{k-1})\widehat{\eta}_{x}^{k}}{(\eta_{x}^{k}\eta_{x}^{k-1})^{2}}.

Notice that

\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{\eta}_{x}^{k}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}\leq(t-\tau)\int_{\tau}^{t}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k}(s)\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Combining this estimate with
(
5.29
) yields

(5.31)

|\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}(t)|_{2}^{2}\leq C(t-\tau)\int_{\tau}^{t}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k}(s)\big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

Multiplying (
5.30
) by

\widehat{U}^{k+1}
, integrating over
I
, and using
(
5.27
) and
(
5.31
), we obtain

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{k+1}\big|_{2}^{2}+c\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}_{x}^{k+1}\big|_{2}^{2}\leq C\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widehat{U}^{k+1}\big|_{2}^{2}+C|\mathcal{R}_{1}^{k}|_{2}^{2}.

It follows from Grönwall’s inequality that

(5.32)

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(t)\leq C(t-\tau)^{2}e^{C(t-\tau)}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(t)\quad\text{for all }t\in[\tau,\tau+T].

We decrease
T
once more so that

CT^{2}e^{CT}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

It follows that

\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k+1}(\tau+T)\leq\frac{1}{2}\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(\tau+T),\qquad\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\big(\widehat{\mathcal{E}}^{k}(\tau+T)\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty,

which is similar to (
5.8
).

Finally, following an argument similar to the rest of
Step 2
in the proof of Theorem
1.1
, we can obtain the existence of the solution
(U,\eta)
to problem (
5.18
).

Step 4: Uniqueness, time continuity, and (
1.22
)–(
1.23
).
The uniqueness and the time continuity follow by the same argument as in
Step 3
of the proof of Theorem
1.1
. We only sketch the proof of (
1.22
)–(
1.23
) in this case.

By
\eqref{secondreformulationz}_{1}
, for

(t,x)\in(\tau,\tau+T]\times I
,

U_{t}=\frac{1}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\Big(U_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U_{x}-\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\Big)-\frac{2\eta_{xx}(U_{x}-\rho_{0})}{\eta_{x}^{3}}.

Equivalently,

\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U_{x}=\alpha\eta_{x}^{2}U_{t}-\alpha U_{xx}+2\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}+\frac{2\alpha\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}}(U_{x}-\rho_{0}).

Using the calculation similar to (
5.15
), together with
Lemma
A.4
and (
5.26
)–(
5.27
), every term on the right-hand side of the above is bounded uniformly on

(\tau,\tau+T]\times I
. Hence,

|\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U_{x}|_{\infty}\leq C.

Since
d(x)\sim\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\in H^{3}(I)\subset C^{1}(\bar{I})
, this implies

|U_{x}(t,x)|\leq Cd(x)\quad\text{for all }(t,x)\in(\tau,\tau+T]\times\bar{I},\qquad U_{x}\big|_{(\tau,\tau+T]\times\Gamma}=0.

Together with the compatibility estimate for
(u_{*})_{x}
obtained in

Step 1
, this also holds at
t=\tau
. Consequently,
(
1.22
)–(
1.23
) hold with
(0,T_{*}]
replaced by

(\tau,\tau+T]
.

Finally, taking
T_{*}=T
, we obtain all the conclusions of Theorem
5.1
.
∎

6.
Global-in-time boundedness of the effective velocity and
\eta_{x}

We will give the proof for the global existence and uniqueness results stated in Theorem
1.2
in §6-§8. According to Theorem
1.1
ii), there exists a unique local-in-time classical solution
U
in
[0,T_{*}]\times\bar{I}
to the problem (
1.16
) for some positive time
T_{*}
, which satisfies (
1.25
) and the homogeneous Neumann boundary condition,
U_{x}(t,x)|_{\Gamma}=0
for
t\in[0,T_{*}]
. Let
\overline{T}_{*}
denote the maximal existence time of the above
classical solution. Hereinafter,
0<T<\overline{T}_{*}
is arbitrary.
The constants
C(\cdots,T)
in the following a priori estimates depend only on
(\rho_{0},u_{0},\varepsilon_{0},\alpha,|I|,T)
and can be chosen
increasing in
T
and finite for every finite
T
.

6.1.
The lower bound of
\eta_{x}

First, the so-called effective velocity is defined as follows.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
U
,
H
,
\eta
,
\rho_{0}
,
\alpha
be defined as in §
1
.
V
is said to be the effective velocity if

(6.1)

V=U+\frac{H_{x}}{\rho_{0}}=U+\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}}{\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{x}}-\frac{\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}.

Next, we give the fundamental energy estimates and BD entropy estimates.

Lemma 6.1
.

For any
T>0
, it holds that for all
0\leq t\leq T
,

•

fundamental energy estimates
(0<\alpha\leq 1)

(6.2)

\int\Big(\rho_{0}U^{2}+\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{x}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int\frac{\rho_{0}U_{x}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\leq C;

•

BD entropy estimates
(0<\alpha<1)

(6.3)

\int\Big(\rho_{0}\Big|U+\frac{H_{x}}{\rho_{0}}\Big|^{2}+\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{x}}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int\frac{H_{x}^{2}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}s\leq C.

Proof.

For simplicity, we only give the proof of the BD entropy estimates. It follows from the equation
\eqref{lagrange}_{1}
by applying
\partial_{x}
that

H_{tx}+\left(H\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\right)_{x}=0,

which, together with
\eqref{lagrange}_{2}
and (
6.1
), leads to

(6.4)

\rho_{0}V_{t}+(H^{2})_{x}=0.

Thus, multiplying (
6.4
) by
V
and integrating the resulting equality yield that

(6.5)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\Big(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{0}V^{2}+\eta_{x}H^{2}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int\frac{H_{x}^{2}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x=0.

Integrating above over
[0,T]
gives the desired conclusion.
∎

Remark 6.1
.

It follows from (
1.8
) that
\rho_{0}
satisfies the initial requirement of the BD entropy estimate when
0<\alpha<1
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\int\rho_{0}(\log\rho_{0})_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha^{2}}\int\rho_{0}^{1-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int d(x)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C.

Clearly, by (
6.1
) and (
6.4
), one can deduce the following corollary.

Corollary 6.1
.

The effective velocity
V
satisfies the following equation:

(6.6)

V_{t}+2H(V-U)=0.

Based on the above discussions, one can derive the upper bound of depth.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
T>0
and
0<\alpha\leq 1
, it holds that

\left|H(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq C\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T.

Proof.

Integrating
\eqref{secondreformulation}_{1}
over
(0,x)
for
x\in I
shows that

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}U\,\mathrm{d}z-\frac{\rho_{0}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}+\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}=0,

which, along with
\eqref{lagrange}_{1}
, yields

(6.7)

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}U\,\mathrm{d}z+H\right)+\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}=0.

Then, integrating above over
[0,t]
gives

(6.8)

\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}U(t,z)\,\mathrm{d}z+H(t,x)\leq\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}u_{0}\,\mathrm{d}z+\rho_{0}(x).

Finally, it follows from (
6.2
) in Lemma
6.1
that

\left|\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}U(t,z)\,\mathrm{d}z\right|\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U\big|_{2}\leq C\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T,

which, along with (
6.8
), yields the boundedness of
H
.

Thus, the proof of Lemma
6.2
is completed.
∎

Next, we can obtain the
L^{q}
(
2\leq q<\infty
) estimates of the velocity.

Lemma 6.3
.

For any
T>0
,
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
0\leq p<\infty
, it holds that

\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}U(t)\big|_{p+2}^{p+2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{|U|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T.

Proof.

Multiplying
\eqref{secondreformulation}_{1}
by
\left|U\right|^{p}U

(0\leq p<\infty)
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p+2}\left(\rho_{0}\left|U\right|^{p+2}\right)_{t}+\left(\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}\left|U\right|^{p}U}{\eta_{x}^{2}}-\frac{\rho_{0}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(p+1)\left(\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}-\frac{\rho_{0}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)\left|U\right|^{p}U_{x},

which, along with Lemma
6.2
,
H=\frac{\rho_{0}}{\eta_{x}}
, the Hölder and Young inequalities, implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p+2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}U\big|_{p+2}^{p+2}+(p+1)\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(p+1)\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}\left|U\right|^{p}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}x=(p+1)\int H^{2}\left|U\right|^{p}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p)\int\rho_{0}H^{2}\left|U\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{p+1}{8}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p)\left|\rho_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{2}{p+2}}|H|_{\infty}^{2}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}U\big|_{p+2}^{p}+\frac{p+1}{8}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p)+\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}U\big|_{p+2}^{p+2}+\frac{p+1}{8}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}.

Then, it follows from the Grönwall inequality that

(6.9)

\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}U(t)\big|_{p+2}^{p+2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{|U|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(p,T)\big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}u_{0}\big|_{p+2}^{p+2}+1\big),

for all
0\leq t\leq T
. For the initial data, one can get from Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
that

\displaystyle\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}u_{0}\big|^{p+2}_{p+2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\rho_{0}\right|_{\infty}\left|u_{0}\right|_{\infty}^{p+2}\leq C(p),

which, along with (
6.9
), yields the desired estimate in this lemma.

The proof of Lemma
6.3
is completed.
∎

Now, one can get the following lower bound of
\eta_{x}
.

Lemma 6.4
.

For any
T>0
and
0<\alpha\leq 1
, it holds that

\inf_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}}\eta_{x}(t,x)\geq C^{-1}(T)>0.

Proof.

First, define

F(t,x)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}(z)U(t,z)\,\mathrm{d}z,&0\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2},\\[5.16663pt]
\displaystyle-\int_{x}^{1}\rho_{0}(z)U(t,z)\,\mathrm{d}z,&\frac{1}{2}<x\leq 1.\end{cases}

Then integrating
\eqref{secondreformulation}_{1}
over
(0,x)
for
0\leq x\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
(x,1)
for
\frac{1}{2}<x\leq 1
, respectively, and using
\eqref{lagrange}_{1}
, we can obtain an equation similar to (
6.7
):

(6.10)

H_{t}+H^{2}=-F_{t}.

Next, it follows from Lemma
6.3
,
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
, and the Hölder inequality that, for any
q\geq 2
and
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,\frac{1}{2}]
,

(6.11)

|F(t,x)|\leq\Big(\int_{0}^{x}\rho_{0}\,\mathrm{d}z\Big)^{1-\frac{1}{q}}\Big(\int_{I}\rho_{0}|U|^{q}\,\mathrm{d}z\Big)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C(q,T)\rho_{0}^{(1+\alpha)(1-\frac{1}{q})}.

Hence, taking
q=\frac{1+\alpha}{\alpha}
(
q\geq 2
since
0<\alpha\leq 1
) in the above yields

(6.12)

|F(t,x)|\leq C(T)\rho_{0}\qquad\text{for $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[0,\frac{1}{2}]$}.

Similarly, applying the above argument, we can also show that (
6.12
) holds for
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times[\frac{1}{2},1]
. Therefore, we arrive at

(6.13)

|F(t,x)|\leq C(T)\rho_{0}\qquad\text{for $(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}$}.

Finally, integrating (
6.10
) with respect to
t
, we obtain from (
6.13
) that, for any
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\bar{I}
,

H(t,x)=\rho_{0}(x)+F(0,x)-F(t,x)-\underline{\int_{0}^{t}H(s,x)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s}_{\,\geq 0}\leq C(T)\rho_{0},

which, along with equation
H=\frac{\rho_{0}}{\eta_{x}}
, yields the desired uniform lower bound of
\eta_{x}
.
∎

6.2.
L^{p}

(2\leq p\leq\infty)
estimates of the effective velocity

Now, one can derive the
L^{p}
(
2\leq p<\infty
) estimates of
V
.

Lemma 6.5
.

For any
T>0
,
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
r>\frac{p-1}{p}
and
2\leq p<\infty
, it holds that

\left|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(t)\right|_{p}\leq C(r,p,T)\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T.

Proof.

Multiplying (
6.6
) by
\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}
(
r>\frac{p-1}{p}
,
2\leq p<\infty
) yields

\left(\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(t,x)\right)_{t}+2H\left(\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(t,x)\right)=\frac{2\rho_{0}^{r\alpha+1}U(t,x)}{\eta_{x}(t,x)}.

Then, one can solve the above ODE to deduce that

(6.14)

\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(t,x)=\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(0,x)e^{-\int_{0}^{t}2H(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{2\rho_{0}^{r\alpha+1}U(\tau,x)}{\eta_{x}(\tau,x)}e^{-\int_{\tau}^{t}2H(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Taking the
L^{p}
-norm (
2\leq p<\infty
) of both sides of (
6.14
), one gets from
H\geq 0
, Lemmas
6.3
-
6.4
and the Minkowski integral inequality that

(6.15)

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(t)\right|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(0)\right|_{p}+2\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{r\alpha+1}U}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{p}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(0)\right|_{p}+C(T)\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{r+\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\alpha p}}\int_{0}^{t}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{p}}U\big|_{p}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(0)\right|_{p}+C(r,p,T).

For the initial data, since
(r-1)p>-1
, one has

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}V(0)\right|_{p}

\displaystyle=\Big|\rho_{0}^{r\alpha}\left(u_{0}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}}{\rho_{0}^{\alpha}}\right)\Big|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{r}\left|u_{0}\right|_{p}+\big|\rho_{0}^{(r-1)\alpha}\big|_{p}\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(r)\widetilde{E}(0,U)+C\left(\int d(x)^{(r-1)p}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq C(r,p),

which, along with (
6.15
), yields the desired estimate in this lemma.

The proof of Lemma
6.5
is completed.
∎

Finally, one can obtain the weighted
L^{\infty}
-boundedness of
V
.

Lemma 6.6
.

For any
T>0
and
0<\alpha\leq 1
, it holds that

\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq C(T)\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T.

Proof.

According to Lemmas
6.4
and
6.5
, Lemma
A.4
and Hölder’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\alpha}U}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}HU|_{\infty}\leq C\big(|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}HU|_{1}+\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}HU\right|_{1}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}H_{x}U\right|_{1}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}HU_{x}\right|_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{\alpha+1}U}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{1}+\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{1}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha+1}(V-U)U\right|_{1}+\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{\alpha+1}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\Big(\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}+\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\alpha+1}{2}}V\big|_{2}\Big)\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\Big(|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}|_{\infty}|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}U|_{2}^{2}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{1+\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}\Big)\leq C(T)\Big(1+\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}\Big).

Integrating above over
[0,t]
, along with (
6.2
), implies that

(6.16)

\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\alpha}U}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(T)\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T.

Next, set
r=1
in (
6.14
). Then it holds that

\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(t,x)=\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(0,x)e^{-\int_{0}^{t}2H(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t}\frac{2\rho_{0}^{1+\alpha}U(\tau,x)}{\eta_{x}(\tau,x)}e^{-\int_{\tau}^{t}2H(s,x)\,\mathrm{d}s}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Taking the
L^{\infty}
-norm of both sides of the above equality, then one can get from (
6.16
) that

(6.17)

\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(t)\right|_{\infty}\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(0)\right|_{\infty}+2\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\alpha}U}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(0)\right|_{\infty}+C(T).

For the initial data, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}V(0)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\Big|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\left(u_{0}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}}{\rho_{0}^{\alpha}}\right)\Big|_{\infty}\leq C\left(\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}\left|u_{0}\right|_{\infty}+\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\widetilde{E}(0,U)+C\leq C.

The proof of Lemma
6.6
is completed.
∎

6.3.
The upper bound of
\eta_{x}

This subsection is devoted to showing the upper bound of
\eta_{x}
.
First, one needs to refine the power of weights in Lemma
6.3
.

Lemma 6.7
.

For any
T>0
,
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
\beta>-\alpha
and
0\leq p<\infty
, it holds that

\begin{gathered}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}\left|U\right|^{p}(t,x)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C(\beta,p,T).\end{gathered}

Proof.

According to Lemma
6.3
,
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\in H^{3}
and
\rho_{0}\sim d(x)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}
, it suffices to prove the lemma for the case when
-\alpha<\beta\leq 1
and
0<p<\infty
. The proof is divided into the following two steps.

Step 1:
\beta>0
.

For every
\beta>0
,
0<p<\infty
, and any given
\varepsilon
such that
0<\varepsilon<\min\left\{1,\beta,\frac{p}{2}\right\}
, it follows from Lemma
6.3
and Hölder’s inequality that for all
0\leq t\leq T
,

(6.18)

\displaystyle\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}\left|U\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta-\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{1-\varepsilon}\left(\int\rho_{0}\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{\varepsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\beta-\varepsilon}{\alpha}}\left(\int\rho_{0}\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{\varepsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\varepsilon}\leq C(\beta,p,T).

Step 2:
-\alpha<\beta\leq 0
.

Now, suppose that
\varepsilon,r
are fixed constants depending only on
\alpha,\beta
such that

0<\varepsilon<\alpha-\left|\beta\right|\quad\text{and}\quad 1<r<\frac{\alpha}{\left|\beta\right|+\varepsilon}.

Then, it follows from
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
, (
6.18
) and Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle\int\rho_{0}^{-\left|\beta\right|}\left|U\right|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int\rho_{0}^{-(\left|\beta\right|+\varepsilon)r}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}\left(\int\rho_{0}^{\frac{\varepsilon r}{r-1}}\left|U\right|^{\frac{pr}{r-1}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{r-1}{r}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,p,T)\left(\int d(x)^{-\frac{(\left|\beta\right|+\varepsilon)r}{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}\leq C(\beta,p,T),

for all
0\leq t\leq T
. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
6.7
is completed.
∎

Next, the following lemma deals with the first order estimate of the effective velocity.

Lemma 6.8
.

For any
T>0
,
0<\alpha\leq 1
and
\beta>3\alpha
, it holds that

\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(\beta,T)\ \ \text{for all }0\leq t\leq T.

Proof.

Applying
\rho_{0}^{\beta}V_{x}\partial_{x}
to both sides of (
6.6
) and integrating the resulting equality over
I
, along with (
6.1
), one gets

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}V_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}H(U_{x}-V_{x})V_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}H_{x}(V-U)V_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2\int\rho_{0}^{\beta+1}\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}V_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}HV_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\rho_{0}^{\beta+1}(V-U)^{2}V_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Then it follows from
\beta>3\alpha
, Lemmas
6.2
,
6.5
and
6.7
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}V_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\beta+1}{2\alpha}}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}\big|_{2}+\left|H\right|_{\infty}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta+2}{4}}V\big|_{4}^{2}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}\big|_{2}+\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta+2}{4}}U\big|_{4}^{2}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}\big|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta,T)\Big(1+\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\Big),

which, along with Grönwall’s inequality and Lemma
6.1
, yields that

(6.19)

\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}(t)\big|_{2}\leq C(\beta,T)\big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\beta}{2}}V_{x}(0)\big|_{2}+1\big)\ \ \text{for }0\leq t\leq T.

For the initial data, since
\beta>3\alpha
,
\rho_{0}\sim d(x)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}
and

V_{x}(0,x)=\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}}{\rho_{0}^{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{2}}{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}}+(u_{0})_{x},

it follows that

\displaystyle\int\rho_{0}^{\beta}(V_{x}(0,x))^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\int\big(\rho_{0}^{\beta-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}^{2}+\rho_{0}^{\beta-4\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{4}+\rho_{0}^{\beta}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}\big)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\big|\rho_{0}^{\beta-2\alpha}\big|_{1}+\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}^{4}\big|\rho_{0}^{\beta-4\alpha}\big|_{1}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}\left\|u_{0}\right\|_{1}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\big|d^{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}-2}\big|_{1}+\big|d^{\frac{\beta}{\alpha}-4}\big|_{1}\big)+C(\beta)\leq C(\beta),

which, along with (
6.19
), yields the desired estimate in this lemma.

The proof of Lemma
6.8
is completed.
∎

With the help of Lemmas
6.7
-
6.8
, one can improve the order of
\eta_{x}
in Lemma
6.3
.

Lemma 6.9
.

For any
T>0
,
0<\alpha\leq 1
,
0\leq p<\infty
, it holds that

\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\iota}{p+2}}\eta_{x}^{\frac{1}{p+2}}U(t)\big|_{p+2}^{p+2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\iota}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\sqrt{\eta_{x}}}\Big|_{2}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C(\iota,p,T),

for all
0\leq t\leq T
, where
\iota=0
if
0<\alpha<1
and
\iota>0
if
\alpha=1
.

Proof.

Let
\iota=0
if
0<\alpha<1
and
\iota>0
if
\alpha=1
. Multiplying
\eqref{secondreformulation}_{1}
by
\rho_{0}^{\iota}\eta_{x}\left|U\right|^{p}U
and integrating the resulting equality over
I
give that

(6.20)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p+2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\eta_{x}\left|U\right|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x+(p+1)\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\left|U\right|^{p}U_{x}^{2}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p+2}\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\left|U\right|^{p+2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{\iota}{\alpha}\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}H_{x}\left|U\right|^{p}U\,\mathrm{d}x:=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{J}_{i}.

For
\mathcal{J}_{1}
, one deduces from integration by parts and Lemma
6.7
that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{J}_{1}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p+2}\Big|\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\left|U\right|^{p+2}U_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big|=\frac{1}{(p+2)(p+3)}\Big|\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\big(U\left|U\right|^{p+2}\big)_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big|

(6.21)

\displaystyle=\frac{1+\iota}{\alpha(p+2)(p+3)}\Big|\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U\left|U\right|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\iota,p)\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\iota-\alpha}{p+3}}U\big|_{p+3}^{p+3}\leq C(\iota,p,T).

For
\mathcal{J}_{2}
, it follows from Lemmas
6.4
,
6.7
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

(6.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\iota}{\alpha}\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\iota)\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\iota}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\sqrt{\eta_{x}}}\Big|_{2}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\iota-2\alpha}{p+2}}\eta_{x}^{-\frac{1}{p+2}}U\big|_{p+2}^{\frac{p+2}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\iota,p,T)+\frac{1}{8}\Big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{1+\iota}{2}}\frac{\left|U\right|^{\frac{p}{2}}U_{x}}{\sqrt{\eta_{x}}}\Big|_{2}^{2}.

For
\mathcal{J}_{3}
, it follows from (
6.1
) that

(6.23)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}(V-U)\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{\alpha}\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}V|U|^{p}UU_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1}{\alpha}\int\frac{\rho_{0}^{1+\iota-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\left|U\right|^{p}UU_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\,\mathrm{d}x-(p+2)\mathcal{J}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{J}_{31}+\mathcal{J}_{32}-(p+2)\mathcal{J}_{1}.

For
\mathcal{J}_{31}
, letting
0<\varepsilon<1-\alpha+\iota
, one gets from integration by parts, Lemmas
6.5
and
6.7
-
6.8
, Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality that

(6.24)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{31}

\displaystyle=\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}V|U|^{p}UU_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x=\frac{1}{p+2}\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}V\left(|U|^{p+2}\right)_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{p+2}\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota}V_{x}\left|U\right|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{1+\iota}{\alpha(p+2)}\int\rho_{0}^{1+\iota-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}V\left|U\right|^{p+2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C(\iota,p)\big(\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{3\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}V_{x}\big|_{2}+\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}V\big|_{2}\big)\big|\rho_{0}^{\frac{2-3\alpha+2\iota-2\varepsilon}{2p+4}}U\big|_{2p+4}^{p+2}
