Finally, for every finite
T>0
, the cited estimates, combined with
the local regularity conclusions of Theorem
1.1
ii)
and Theorem
5.1
on overlapping time intervals, yield
(
1.27
) and (
1.22
)-(
1.23
) and (
1.26
) with
T_{*}
replaced by
T
.
Uniqueness follows from the corresponding
local uniqueness assertion. This completes the proof of
Theorem
1.2
.

8.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.4

Based on the proof in §
5.2
, one can deduce from Theorem
1.2
and (
E.3
) that Theorem
1.4
holds.

9.
Non-existence of global solutions with
L^{\infty}
decay on the velocity

This section will be devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.5
. For some positive time
T
, let
(\rho,u)(t,y)
in
\overline{\mathbb{I}(T)}
be the global classical solution obtained in Theorem
1.4
. Define the following physical quantities:

\displaystyle m(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{I(t)}\rho(t,y)\,\mathrm{d}y\ \ \textrm{(total mass)},\ \ \mathbb{P}(t)=\int_{I(t)}\rho(t,y)u(t,y)\,\mathrm{d}y\ \ \textrm{(momentum)},

\displaystyle E_{k}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{I(t)}\rho(t,y)u^{2}(t,y)\,\mathrm{d}y\ \ \textrm{(total kinetic energy)}.

First, one shows that
(\rho,u)
satisfies the
laws of conservation of
m(t)
and
\mathbb{P}(t)
.

Lemma 9.1
.

For any
T>0
, it holds that

m(t)=m(0),\ \ \mathbb{P}(t)=\mathbb{P}(0)\ \ and\ \ E_{k}(t)<\infty\ \ {\rm{for}}\quad t\in[0,T].

Proof.

First, one can obtain that

(9.1)

|\mathbb{P}(t)|=\Big|\int_{I(t)}\rho u\,\mathrm{d}y\Big|\leq\sup_{y\in I(t)}|u(t,y)|\int_{I(t)}\rho\,\mathrm{d}y<\infty.

Second, let
I(t)=(a(t),b(t))
. The momentum equation
\eqref{shallow}_{2}
implies

(9.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{I(t)}(\rho u)_{t}\,\mathrm{d}y+b^{\prime}(t)\rho u\big|_{y=b(t)}-a^{\prime}(t)\rho u\big|_{y=a(t)}

\displaystyle=\int_{I(t)}(\rho u_{y}-\rho u^{2}-\rho^{2})_{y}\,\mathrm{d}y=0,

where we have used the facts that
(\rho,\rho u^{2},\rho u_{y})
vanish on the moving boundary.

The conservation of the total mass and the boundedness of the kinetic energy can be proved via the similar argument. The proof of Lemma
9.1
is completed.
∎

Then it follows from the definitions of
m(t)
,
\mathbb{P}(t)
and
E_{k}(t)
that

|\mathbb{P}(t)|\leq\int_{I(t)}\rho(t,y)|u(t,y)|\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\sqrt{2m(t)E_{k}(t)},

which, along with Lemma
9.1
, implies that

0<\frac{|\mathbb{P}(0)|^{2}}{2m(0)}\leq E_{k}(t)\leq\frac{1}{2}m(0)\sup_{y\in I(t)}|u(t,y)|^{2}\quad\text{for}\quad t\in[0,T].

Therefore, the proof of Theorem
1.5
is completed.

Appendix A
Some basic lemmas

For the convenience of readers, we list some basic facts which have been used frequently in this paper. Throughout Appendices A-E, let
I=(0,1)
,
\Gamma=\partial I
,
d=d(x)=\text{dist}(x,\Gamma)
be the distance function from
x\in\bar{I}
to
\Gamma
. Let
0\leq\phi_{0}=\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
satisfying
\phi_{0}\in H^{3}
be defined as in §
1
, and
\left|I\right|
denotes the Lebesgue measure of
I
.

The first one is on the separability and density of the weighted Sobolev spaces.

Lemma A.1
.

[
32
]

Let
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
s>0
. Then

\mathrm{i)}

H^{k}_{d^{s}}
is a reflexive separable Banach space;

\mathrm{ii)}

C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
is dense in
H^{k}_{d^{s}}
with respect to the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{k,d^{s}}
for
k\geq 0
.

The next lemma concerns the well-known interpolation inequality of Sobolev spaces.

Lemma A.2
.

[
33
]

Suppose that
F\in H^{p}\cap H^{q}
for
p,q\geq 0
. Then
F\in H^{s}
for all
s=p\varepsilon+q(1-\varepsilon)
and
0\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
, and the following inequality holds,

\left\|F\right\|_{s}\leq C(p,q,\varepsilon)\left\|F\right\|_{p}^{\varepsilon}\left\|F\right\|_{q}^{1-\varepsilon},

where
C(p,q,\varepsilon)>0
is a constant depending only on
(p,q,\varepsilon)
.

The third lemma gives some weighted interpolation inequalities, which are useful for the analysis in the current paper.

Lemma A.3
.

It holds that for all
k>-1
and all
F\in H^{1}_{d^{k+1}}
,

(A.1)

\big|d^{\frac{k}{2}}F\big|_{2}\leq C\left(k\right)\left(\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F\big|_{2}+\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F_{x}\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

and, as a consequence, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

(A.2)

\big|d^{\frac{k}{2}}F\big|_{2}\leq C(k,\varepsilon)\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F\big|_{2}+\varepsilon\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F_{x}\big|_{2}.

Here,
C(k)
and
C(k,\varepsilon)
are positive constants depending only on
(k,\left|I\right|)
and
(k,\varepsilon,\left|I\right|)
, respectively. In particular, the above conclusions still hold when
d
is replaced by
\phi_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof for
k=0
can be found in
[
35
,
36
]
, and now we give the proof for the general
k>-1
.
First, we consider the case that
F\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
. Based on the symmetry of the distance function, in order to obtain (
A.1
), it suffices to derive the following inequality,

(A.3)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C(k)\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+C(k)\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Actually, it follows from integration by parts that

(A.4)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=&\frac{2^{-k-1}}{k+1}F^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{2}{k+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}FF_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x,\\
\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x=&\frac{2^{-k-2}}{k+2}F^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{2}{k+2}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+2}FF_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

which, along with Hölder’s inequality, leads to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{2k+4}{k+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2}{k+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}(2x-1)x^{k+1}FF_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C(k)\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)+C(k)\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}x^{k+1}F_{x}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The proof of (
A.3
) is completed.

Next we consider the case that
F\in H^{1}_{d^{k+1}}
. According to Lemma
A.1
, for every
F\in H^{1}_{d^{k+1}}
, there exists a sequence
\{F^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
, such that

(A.5)

\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F^{\delta}-d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F\big|_{2}+\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F_{x}^{\delta}-d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F_{x}\big|_{2}\to 0\quad\text{as }\delta\to 0.

Then, by (
A.1
), for any
\delta,\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\big|d^{\frac{k}{2}}F^{\delta}-d^{\frac{k}{2}}F^{\varepsilon}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F^{\delta}-d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F^{\varepsilon}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle+C\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F^{\delta}-d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F^{\varepsilon}\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big|d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F_{x}^{\delta}-d^{\frac{k+1}{2}}F_{x}^{\varepsilon}\big|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\to 0,

as
(\delta,\varepsilon)\to(0,0)
, which implies that
\{d^{\frac{k}{2}}F^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{2}
, and hence converges to some limit
G
in
L^{2}
. However, by (
A.5
),
F^{\delta}
converges to
F
in
L^{1}_{\text{loc}}
; hence one can extract a subsequence
F^{\delta_{\ell}}
which converges to
F
a.e. in
I
. Therefore one has
G=d^{\frac{k}{2}}F
. The proof of (
A.1
) for
F\in H^{1}_{d^{k+1}}
is completed.

Finally, it follows from (
A.1
) and Young’s inequality that (
A.2
) holds.
∎

The fourth lemma is the classical Sobolev embedding theorem.

Lemma A.4
.

[
33
]

It holds that

\displaystyle\left|F\right|_{\infty}\leq s_{0}\left|F\right|_{1}+C\left|F_{x}\right|_{1}\ \ \text{for all }F\in W^{1,1};

\displaystyle\left|F\right|_{\infty}\leq s_{0}\left|F\right|_{2}+C\left|F_{x}\right|_{2}\ \ \text{for all }F\in H^{1},

where
s_{0}
and
C
are positive constants depending only on
\left|I\right|
. In particular,
W^{1,1},H^{1}\hookrightarrow C(\bar{I})
continuously. Moreover, if
F|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
, one can choose
s_{0}=0
.

The fifth one is on the Hardy inequalities.

Lemma A.5
.

[
12
,
32
]

It holds that

(A.6)

\displaystyle\big|d^{\frac{k}{2}}F\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(k)\big|d^{\frac{k}{2}+1}(F,F_{x})\big|_{2}\ \

\displaystyle\text{for all }F\in H^{1}_{d^{k+2}}\text{ and }k>-1;

(A.7)

\displaystyle|d^{k}F|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C(k,\varepsilon)\big|d^{k+\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon}(F,F_{x})\big|_{2}\ \

\displaystyle\text{for all }F\in H^{1}_{d^{2k+3-2\varepsilon}}\text{ and }k+1>\varepsilon>0;

(A.8)

\displaystyle|d^{k}F|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C(k)\big|d^{k+\frac{1}{2}}(F,F_{x})\big|_{2}\ \

\displaystyle\text{for all }F\in H^{1}_{d^{2k+1}}\text{ and }k>0,

where
C(k)
and
C(k,\varepsilon)
are constants depending only on
(k,\left|I\right|)
and
(k,\varepsilon,\left|I\right|)
, respectively.
If, additionally,
\bar{d}(x)\in H^{3}
and
\bar{d}(x)\sim d(x)
,
F|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
, then for all
F\in H^{s+1}
,
s=0,1,2
, one has

(A.9)

\|\bar{d}^{-1}F\|_{s}\leq C\left\|F\right\|_{s+1},

where
C
is a constant depending only on
(\bar{d},s,\left|I\right|)
. In particular, (
A.6
)-(
A.9
) still hold when
d
and
\bar{d}
are replaced by
\phi_{0}
.

Proof.

The proof for (
A.6
) can be found in Chapter 1 of Kufner
[
32
]
. (
A.9
) can be proved by following the proof of Lemma 3.1 in
[
12
]
. For any
k+1>\varepsilon>0
, (
A.7
) can be derived from (
A.6
) by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle|d^{k}F|_{1}\leq\big|d^{-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\big|_{2}\big|d^{k+\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}F\big|_{2}\leq C(k,\varepsilon)\big|d^{k+\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon}(F,F_{x})\big|_{2}.

For (
A.8
), according to Lemma
A.4
, (
A.6
), Hölder’s inequality and Young’s inequality, for all
k>0
,

\displaystyle|d^{k}F|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C|d^{2k}F^{2}|_{1}+C|\big(d^{2k}F^{2}\big)_{x}|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C|d^{k}F|_{2}^{2}+C\big|d^{k-\frac{1}{2}}F\big|_{2}^{2}+C\big|d^{k-\frac{1}{2}}F\big|_{2}\big|d^{k+\frac{1}{2}}F_{x}\big|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(k)\big(\big|d^{k+\frac{1}{2}}F\big|_{2}^{2}+\big|d^{k+\frac{1}{2}}F_{x}\big|_{2}^{2}\big).

Thus, the proof is completed.
∎

The sixth one is on some basic properties of the Hilbert basis
\{e_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
of
H^{1}
, and the corresponding proof can be found in Chapter 9 of
[
53
]
.

Lemma A.6
.

[
53
]

Let
\{e_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
be the Hilbert basis of
H^{1}
, which is constructed by solving the eigenvalue problem
-\Delta e+e=\lambda e
with the Neumann boundary condition
e_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
. Then
\{e_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is orthonormal in
L^{2}
and orthogonal in
H^{1}
,
e_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
for
j\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, and

\mathrm{i)}

for all
f\in L^{2}
, it holds that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle f,e_{j}\rangle e_{j}\to f\ \ \text{in }L^{2};

\mathrm{ii)}

for all
f\in H^{1}
, it holds that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\langle f,e_{j}\rangle e_{j}\to f\ \ \text{in }H^{1}.

Here,
\langle f,g\rangle
stands for the
L^{2}
-inner product of functions
f
and
g
, i.e.,
\langle f,g\rangle:=\int fg\,\mathrm{d}x
.

In addition, to get the weighted time continuity for the velocity in our analysis, one needs the following evolution triple embedding.

Lemma A.7
.

Let
T>0
,
s>0
,
F\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})
and
\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})
. Then,
\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\in C([0,T];L^{2})
, and the mapping
t\mapsto\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}
is absolutely continuous, with

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}=2\left<\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t},F\right>_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}\quad\text{for a.e. $t\in(0,T)$}.

Furthermore, it holds that

\big\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\big\|_{C_{t}(L^{2})}\leq C(T)\left\|F\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}+\left\|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\right\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}.

Proof.

Step 1.
We first establish the weighted time continuity by regularization in time. Here,
\phi_{0}^{s}F
is viewed as an
H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}
-valued function by integration against elements of
H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}
. By the definition of the dual norm and the Hölder inequality, one has

\|\phi_{0}^{s}F\|_{-1,\phi_{0}^{s}}\leq\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\big|_{2}\leq\|F\|_{1,\phi_{0}^{s}}.

Since
\phi_{0}
is independent of
t
, the assumptions imply that

\phi_{0}^{s}F\in H^{1}([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})\subset C([0,T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}).

Thus, the endpoint traces of
\phi_{0}^{s}F
are well defined in
H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}
.

Next, we extend
F
to
(-T,2T)
by reflection, setting
F(t,x)=F(-t,x)
for
-T<t<0
and
F(t,x)=F(2T-t,x)
for
T<t<2T
, and retain the same notation. In particular, the extension satisfies

F\in L^{2}([-T,2T];H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}),\qquad\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\in L^{2}([-T,2T];H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}).

Let
\omega_{\delta}
be a standard time mollifier supported in
(-\delta,\delta)
, and set
F^{\delta}=F*\omega_{\delta}
on
[0,T]
for
0<\delta<\frac{T}{2}
. Then
F^{\delta}\in C^{\infty}([0,T];H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})
, and
\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}^{\delta}=(\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t})*\omega_{\delta}
. It follows from the approximation properties of convolution that

\|F^{\delta}-F\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}+\|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}^{\delta}-\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\to 0\quad\text{as }\delta\to 0,

where the time norms are taken over
(0,T)
.

Now, for any
0<\varepsilon,\delta<\frac{T}{2}
, the smoothness of
F^{\delta}
and
F^{\varepsilon}
gives

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}(F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}=2\bigl\langle\phi_{0}^{s}(F_{t}^{\delta}-F_{t}^{\varepsilon})(t),(F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon})(t)\bigr\rangle_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}.

Integrating this identity from any
\tau\in[0,T]
to
t\in[0,T]
and estimating the time integral over
(0,T)
, one obtains

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}(F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}\leq\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}(F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon})(\tau)\big|_{2}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{T}\|\phi_{0}^{s}(F_{t}^{\delta}-F_{t}^{\varepsilon})\|_{-1,\phi_{0}^{s}}\|F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon}\|_{1,\phi_{0}^{s}}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Integrating the above inequality with respect to
\tau
over
(0,T)
, dividing by
T
, and applying Hölder’s inequality yield

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}(F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon})(t)\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}(F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{t}(L^{2})}^{2}+2\|\phi_{0}^{s}(F_{t}^{\delta}-F_{t}^{\varepsilon})\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\|F^{\delta}-F^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\to 0

as
(\varepsilon,\delta)\to(0,0)
. Thus,
\{\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F^{\delta}\}
is a Cauchy family in
C([0,T];L^{2})
. Since it also converges to
\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F
in
L^{2}([0,T];L^{2})
, its uniform limit agrees with
\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
. Hence,

\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\in C([0,T];L^{2}),\qquad\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F^{\delta}\to\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\quad\text{in }C([0,T];L^{2}).

Next, for every
t,\tau\in[0,T]
, the same calculation for
F^{\delta}
gives

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F^{\delta}(t)\big|_{2}^{2}=\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F^{\delta}(\tau)\big|_{2}^{2}+2\int_{\tau}^{t}\bigl\langle\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}^{\delta},F^{\delta}\bigr\rangle_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}.

Moreover, by Hölder’s inequality and the convergence of the regularizations,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\bigl|\langle\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}^{\delta},F^{\delta}\rangle_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}-\langle\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t},F\rangle_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}\bigr|\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}^{\delta}-\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\|F^{\delta}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}+\|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\|F^{\delta}-F\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\to 0.

Consequently, letting
\delta\to 0
in the energy identity for
F^{\delta}
yields

(A.10)

\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}=\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(\tau)\big|_{2}^{2}+2\int_{\tau}^{t}\bigl\langle\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t},F\bigr\rangle_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}

for all
t,\tau\in[0,T]
. The integrand belongs to
L^{1}(0,T)
by the assumptions and Hölder’s inequality. Hence,
t\mapsto\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}
is absolutely continuous, and

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}=2\bigl\langle\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t},F\bigr\rangle_{H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}\times H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}}}\quad\text{for a.e. }t\in(0,T).

Finally, integrating (
A.10
) with respect to
\tau
over
(0,T)
and applying Hölder’s inequality, we deduce that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\|_{L^{2}_{t}(L^{2})}^{2}+2\|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}.

Since
\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F\big|_{2}\leq\|F\|_{1,\phi_{0}^{s}}
, Young’s inequality gives

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big|\phi_{0}^{\frac{s}{2}}F(t)\big|_{2}^{2}\leq(1+T^{-1})\|F\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}^{2}+\|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}(H^{-1}_{\phi_{0}^{s}})}^{2},

which yields the asserted estimate with
C(T)=(1+T^{-1})^{\frac{1}{2}}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Finally, in §
3
, one needs the following well-known Hahn-Banach Theorem.

Lemma A.8
.

[
7
]

Suppose that
X
is a normed vector space and
Y\subset X
is a linear subspace. If
g:Y\to\mathbb{R}
is a continuous linear functional and
Y
is dense in
X
, then there exists a unique
\bar{g}\in X^{*}
that extends
g
, namely,
\bar{g}(x)=g(x)
, for all
x\in Y
, satisfying

\left\|\bar{g}\right\|_{X^{*}}=\sup_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle x\in Y}}{{\left\|x\right\|_{X}\leq 1}}}\left|g(x)\right|=\left\|g\right\|_{Y^{*}}.

Appendix B
Remarks on the compatibility conditions

This appendix is devoted to giving one equivalent form of the initial condition (
1.19
) or (
1.24
) in terms of
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
themselves and their spatial derivatives.

First, by (
1.16
), one has that all the desired initial values of time derivatives of
U
can be expressed by those of
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
themselves and their spatial derivatives, namely,

(B.1)

\displaystyle U_{t}(0,x)

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x},

\displaystyle U_{tx}(0,x)

\displaystyle=\Big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\Big)_{x},

\displaystyle U_{txx}(0,x)

\displaystyle=\Big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\Big)_{xx},

\displaystyle U_{tt}(0,x)

\displaystyle=\rho_{0}^{-1}\Big(\rho_{0}U_{tx}(0,x)-2\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}+2\rho_{0}^{2}(u_{0})_{x}\Big)_{x}.

Second, (
B.1
) implies
(u_{0})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
. Indeed, we obtain from
\eqref{1..16}_{1}
that

(B.2)

U_{t}(0,x)=(u_{0})_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(u_{0})_{x}-\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}.

Then, if
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, taking the
L^{\infty}
norm, (
1.8
), (
1.19
), and Lemmas
A.4
-
A.5
give

\displaystyle|\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(u_{0})_{x}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\left|U_{t}(0)\right|_{\infty}+\left|(u_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\right|_{\infty}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{\alpha}}\left|(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\right|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq CE(0,U)+C\leq C,

which, along with
(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}\neq 0
, yields

(B.3)

\left|(u_{0})_{x}(x)\right|\leq Cd(x)\quad\text{for all }x\in\bar{I}.

If
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
, one can obtain (
B.3
) by repeating the above calculation with
E(0,U)
replaced by
\widetilde{E}(0,U)
.

Next, based on (
B.1
)-(
B.3
), we give the following auxiliary lemma to give one equivalent form of (
1.19
) or (
1.24
) in terms of
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
themselves and their spatial derivatives.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
be the initial data of (
1.16
).
Then, when
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
,
E(0,U)<\infty
if and only if

(B.4)

\begin{gathered}(u_{0})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0,\quad u_{0}\in H^{4}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}},\quad\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\in H^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}},\\
\rho_{0}^{-1}\Big(\rho_{0}\big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\big)_{x}\Big)_{x}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}};\end{gathered}

when
\frac{1}{3}<\alpha\leq 1
,
\widetilde{E}(0,U)<\infty
if and only if

(B.5)

\begin{gathered}(u_{0})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0,\quad u_{0}\in H^{4}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\alpha}},\quad\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\in H^{1}_{\rho_{0}}\cap H^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\alpha}},\\
\rho_{0}^{-1}\Big(\rho_{0}\big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\big)_{x}-2\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}\Big)_{x}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}}.\end{gathered}

Proof.

For brevity, we only give the proof for (
B.4
), and (
B.5
) can be derived similarly. If
E(0,U)<\infty
, clearly, by (
B.1
)-(
B.3
), we have

(B.6)

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0,\quad u_{0}\in H^{4}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}},\quad\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\in H^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}},

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{-1}\Big(\rho_{0}\big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\big)_{x}-2\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}+2\rho_{0}^{2}(u_{0})_{x}\Big)_{x}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}}.

Since
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
, it follows from
u_{0}\in H^{4}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}}
,
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
, and Lemma
A.5
that

(B.7)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}\big)_{x}=\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}+2(u_{0})_{x}(u_{0})_{xx}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}},

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}^{2}(u_{0})_{x}\big)_{x}=\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(u_{0})_{x}+\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{xx}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}}.

Conversely, if (
B.4
) holds, then we can first obtain (
B.7
) from
u_{0}\in H^{4}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}}
,
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
, and Lemma
A.5
, and then deduce (
B.6
). Hence,
E(0,U)<\infty
.
∎

Finally, based on Lemma
B.1
, we show that the example of the initial data
(\rho_{0},u_{0})
given by (
1.35
) (or (
1.36
)) in Remark
1.3
satisfies the initial conditions (
1.8
) and (
1.19
) (or (
1.24
)) in Theorem
1.1
.

Remark B.1
.

To check that (
1.35
) satisfies the initial conditions (
1.8
) and (
1.19
), we only need to notice that
0<\alpha\leq\frac{1}{3}
and

\rho_{0}^{-1}(\rho_{0}^{2})_{x}\in H^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}},\qquad\rho_{0}^{-1}\Big(\rho_{0}\big(\rho_{0}^{-1}(\rho_{0}^{2})_{x}\big)_{x}\Big)_{x}\in L^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{2\alpha}}.

Next, we check that (
1.36
) satisfies the initial conditions (
1.8
) and (
1.24
). Clearly,
\rho_{0}
satisfies (
1.8
); it suffices to prove (
B.5
). First, via direct calculations, one has

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{x}

\displaystyle=(f_{0})_{x}+\rho_{0},\quad(u_{0})_{xx}=(f_{0})_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x},

(B.8)

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{xxx}

\displaystyle=(f_{0})_{xxx}+\frac{(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{2}}\rho_{0}^{1-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{2}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx},

\displaystyle(u_{0})_{xxxx}

\displaystyle=(f_{0})_{xxxx}+\frac{(1-\alpha)(1-2\alpha)}{\alpha^{3}}\rho_{0}^{1-3\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{3}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3(1-\alpha)}{\alpha^{2}}\rho_{0}^{1-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xxx}.

Then, based on
f_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(I)
, (
1.8
), and (
1.18
), one can check that
(u_{0})_{x}|_{x\in\Gamma}=0
and
u_{0}\in H^{4}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\alpha}}
. Next, since
\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}=\rho_{0}(f_{0})_{x}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}

\displaystyle=(f_{0})_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(f_{0})_{x}

\displaystyle\Big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\Big)_{x}

\displaystyle=(f_{0})_{xxx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(f_{0})_{xx}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}(f_{0})_{x}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{2}(f_{0})_{x},

(B.9)

\displaystyle\Big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\Big)_{xx}=

\displaystyle(f_{0})_{xxxx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(f_{0})_{xxx}-\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{2}(f_{0})_{xx}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}(f_{0})_{xx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xxx}(f_{0})_{x}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{3}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}(f_{0})_{x}+\frac{2}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{-3\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{3}(f_{0})_{x},

which, along with the fact
(
notice that
f_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(I)
and
\rho^{\alpha}_{0}\sim d(x)
)
that

(B.10)

|(f_{0})_{xx}|+|(f_{0})_{xxx}|+|(f_{0})_{xxxx}|\leq C(f_{0},\alpha)\rho_{0}^{100(1+\alpha)}\ \ \text{for }x\in\bar{I},

for some constant
C(f_{0},\alpha)>0
, leads to
\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\in H^{1}_{\rho_{0}}\cap H^{2}_{\rho_{0}^{(3-2\varepsilon_{0})\alpha}}
.

Finally, thanks to (
B.8
)-(
B.9
), one gets

(B.11)

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\rho_{0}\big(\rho_{0}^{-1}\big(\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}-\rho_{0}^{2}\big)_{x}\big)_{x}-2\rho_{0}(u_{0})_{x}^{2}\Big)_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\rho_{0}(f_{0})_{xxx}+\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}(f_{0})_{xx}\Big)_{x}

\displaystyle+\rho_{0}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}(f_{0})_{x}-\frac{1}{\alpha}\rho_{0}^{1-2\alpha}(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}^{2}(f_{0})_{x}-2\rho_{0}\big((f_{0})_{x}+\rho_{0}\big)^{2}\Big)_{x}.

We get from
\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\sim d(x)
and (
B.10
) that the right-hand side of
(
B.11
)
belongs to
L^{2}
. This implies that (
B.5
) holds, and hence
\widetilde{E}(0,U)<\infty
.

Appendix C
Cross-derivatives embedding

The following embedding theorem will be frequently used throughout the whole paper.

Proposition C.1
.

Let
s>0
and
\kappa>0
be given constants, such that

(C.1)

\frac{1}{2}<s\leq\frac{\kappa+1}{2},

and
F\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
be a function defined on
I
. If
F
satisfies

(C.2)

\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}+\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F\right|_{2}<\infty,

and
\phi_{0}^{r}F_{x}\in L^{2}
for some
r\in\big[s,\frac{\kappa+1}{2}\big]
, then it holds that

(C.3)

\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}\right|_{2}\leq C\left(\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F\right|_{2}\right),

where
C>0
is a constant depending only on
(s,\kappa,\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},|I|)
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, assume
C>0
is a constant depending only on
(s,\kappa,\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2},|I|)
.

Step 1: Case
F\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
.

We first consider the case when
F\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
. Via integration by parts, it follows from (
C.1
)-(
C.2
), Lemma
A.3
and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}\right|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}^{2}-\kappa^{2}\left|\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}^{2}-\kappa\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}(F^{2})_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}^{2}\underline{-\kappa\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F^{2}\Big|_{x=0}^{x=1}}_{=0}

(C.4)

\displaystyle\underline{+(2s-1-\kappa)\kappa\int\phi_{0}^{2s-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{\leq 0}+\kappa\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}^{2}+\kappa\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\big|\phi_{0}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}F\big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F\right|_{2}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}\right|_{2}^{2},

which yields that

(C.5)

\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}\right|_{2}\leq C\big(\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F\right|_{2}\big).

Step 2: Case
r=s
.

Note that for
F\in H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2s}}
, one can repeat the above calculation, but still needs to check the rationality of integration by parts of
\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}(F^{2})_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x
. Indeed, via Lemma
A.1
, there exists a smooth sequence
\{F^{\delta}\}_{\delta>0}\subset C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
, such that

(C.6)

|\phi_{0}^{s}F^{\delta}-\phi_{0}^{s}F|_{2}+|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}^{\delta}-\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}|_{2}\to 0\ \ \text{as }\delta\to 0,

which, along with Lemma
A.5
, yields

(C.7)

|\phi_{0}^{s-1}F^{\delta}-\phi_{0}^{s-1}F|_{2}+\big|\phi_{0}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}F^{\delta}-\phi_{0}^{s-\frac{1}{2}}F\big|_{\infty}\to 0\ \ \text{as }\delta\to 0.

Thus, according to (
C.6
)-(
C.7
) and integration by parts for
F^{\delta}\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
,

\displaystyle-2\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F^{\delta}F^{\delta}_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x=(2s-1)\int\phi_{0}^{2s-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}(F^{\delta})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{xx}(F^{\delta})^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x,

one has that for
F\in H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{2s}}
,

\displaystyle-2\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}FF_{x}\,\mathrm{d}x=(2s-1)\int\phi_{0}^{2s-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x+\int\phi_{0}^{2s-1}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F^{2}\,\mathrm{d}x.

The proof of (
C.3
) when
r=s
is completed.

Step 3: General case.

For general
r
, it suffices to show the case when
s<r=\frac{\kappa+1}{2}
, since
H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{p}}\subset H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{q}}
whenever
p\leq q
. Note that, in this case, integration by parts in (
C.4
) fails since we do not have
\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\in L^{2}
.

To overcome this difficulty, setting
\iota:=\frac{\kappa+1}{2}-s
and choosing
0<\varepsilon<1
, we first show a variant of Lemma
A.3
, that is,

(C.8)

\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F\Big|_{2}^{2}\leq C(\kappa,s)\bigg(\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F\Big|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F\Big|_{2}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\Big|_{2}\bigg).

Based on the proof in Lemma
A.3
, it suffices to show (
C.8
) holds for
F\in C^{\infty}(\bar{I})
and
\phi_{0}=x
,
0<x\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Via integration by parts, it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa+1}\frac{2^{2\iota}}{(1+2\varepsilon)^{2\iota}2^{\kappa+1}}F^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)-\frac{2}{\kappa+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}FF_{x}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\iota}{\kappa+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa+1}\frac{2^{2\iota}}{(1+2\varepsilon)^{2\iota}2^{\kappa+1}}F^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)-\frac{2}{\kappa+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}FF_{x}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{2\iota}{\kappa+1}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x,

which gives

(C.9)

\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{1}{s}\frac{2^{2\iota}}{(1+2\varepsilon)^{2\iota}2^{\kappa+1}}F^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)-\frac{2}{s}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}FF_{x}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

The same calculation implies that

(C.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa+2}\frac{2^{2\iota}}{(1+2\varepsilon)^{2\iota}2^{\kappa+2}}F^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)-\frac{2}{\kappa+2}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+2}FF_{x}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{2\iota}{\kappa+2}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+2}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Multiplying (
C.10
) by
\frac{2(\kappa+2)}{s}
, then substituting (
C.10
) into (
C.9
) to cancel the constant term, one obtains that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(\kappa+2)}{s}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{4\iota}{s}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+2}F^{2}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\frac{4}{s}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+2}FF_{x}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x-\frac{2}{s}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{x^{\kappa+1}FF_{x}}{(x+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F\Big|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F\Big|_{2}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\Big|_{2}\bigg),

which completes the proof of (
C.8
).

Now, we continue to prove (
C.3
). It follows from multiplying (
C.2
) by
\frac{\phi_{0}^{\iota}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}
that

(C.11)

\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}+\kappa\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa-1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\Big|_{2}\leq\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}<\infty.

It is worth noting that, according to Lemma
A.5
, each term in (
C.11
) is meaningful for every
\varepsilon>0
. Moreover, similar to the density arguments in
Step 2
, integration by parts still holds in this case, that is, for all
F\in H^{1}_{\phi_{0}^{\kappa+1}}
and
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle-2\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa}(\phi_{0})_{x}FF_{x}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\kappa\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa-1}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F^{2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x-2\iota\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F^{2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F^{2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Via integration by parts, one can deduce from (
C.8
), (
C.11
) and Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}+\kappa\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa-1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\Big|_{2}^{2}-\kappa^{2}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa-1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\kappa\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa}(\phi_{0})_{x}FF_{x}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}+\kappa\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa-1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\Big|_{2}^{2}-\kappa^{2}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa-1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\underline{-2\iota\kappa\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F^{2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota+1}}\,\mathrm{d}x}_{\leq 0}+\kappa^{2}\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa-1}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F^{2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\int\frac{\phi_{0}^{\kappa}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F^{2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{2\iota}}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|^{2}_{2}+\kappa\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F\Big|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}^{2}\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F\right|_{2}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{(\kappa+1)/2}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\Big|_{2}^{2},

which yields that

\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\Big|_{2}\leq C\left(\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right|_{2}+\left|(\phi_{0})_{xx}\right|_{\infty}\left|\phi_{0}^{s}F\right|_{2}\right).

Then one can extract a subsequence (still denoted by
\varepsilon
) such that

(C.12)

\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\to\mathcal{Q}\quad\text{weakly in }L^{2}\quad\text{as }\varepsilon\to 0,

for a function
\mathcal{Q}\in L^{2}
, and

\left|\mathcal{Q}\right|_{2}\leq\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\Big|\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\Big|_{2}\leq C.

However, it follows from
F_{x}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
that

(C.13)

\frac{\phi_{0}^{\frac{\kappa+1}{2}}}{(\phi_{0}+\varepsilon)^{\iota}}F_{x}\to\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}\quad\text{in }L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\ \ \text{as }\varepsilon\to 0.

Then, comparing with (
C.12
)-(
C.13
), one gets from the uniqueness of the limits that
\mathcal{Q}=\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Leibniz formula in Sobolev spaces

In this appendix, we give a brief summary about the operations of derivatives that will be used in §
3
.
First, we give the Leibniz formula in Sobolev spaces.

Lemma D.1
.

[
15
]

Let
F\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
G\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
. Then
FG\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, and the Leibniz formula holds, that is,

(FG)_{x}=F_{x}G+FG_{x}\ \ \text{for a.e. }x\in I.

Lemma
D.1
has the following applications.

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose that
\phi_{0}(x)
,
F(t,x)
and
\mathcal{Q}(t,x)
satisfy the following equation:

(D.1)

\phi_{0}^{s}F_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F=\mathcal{Q}\ \ \text{for a.e. }(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I,

where
s,\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{Q},\,F,\,F_{x}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
. It holds that

\mathrm{i)}

if
\mathcal{Q}_{t},\,F_{t}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
, then
F_{tx}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
,

(D.2)

\phi_{0}^{s}F_{tx}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F_{t}=\mathcal{Q}_{t};

\mathrm{ii)}

if
\mathcal{Q}_{x}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for a.e.
t\in(0,T)
, then
F_{xx}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
,

(D.3)

\phi_{0}^{s}F_{xx}+(\kappa+s)\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F_{x}+\kappa(s-1)\phi_{0}^{s-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F+\kappa\phi_{0}^{s-1}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F=\mathcal{Q}_{x}.

Proof.

To get
\mathrm{i)}
, multiplying (
D.1
) by
\phi_{0}^{-s}
, one has

(D.4)

F_{x}=\phi_{0}^{-s}\mathcal{Q}-\kappa\phi_{0}^{-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F,

which, along with the facts that
\mathcal{Q}_{t},\,F_{t}\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
, yields that
F_{x}
has the weak derivative
F_{tx}\in L^{2}_{\rm loc}
and (
D.2
) holds.

To get
\mathrm{ii)}
, since
\mathcal{Q},F\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
,
\phi_{0}\sim d(x)
and
\phi_{0}\in H^{3}
, it follows from Lemma
D.1
that each term in the right hand side of (
D.4
) belongs to
H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and the following identities hold for a.e.
(t,x)\in(0,T)\times I
,

\displaystyle\left(\phi_{0}^{-s}\mathcal{Q}\right)_{x}=-s\phi_{0}^{-s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}\mathcal{Q}+\phi_{0}^{-s}\mathcal{Q}_{x};

\displaystyle\left(\kappa\phi_{0}^{-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F\right)_{x}=-\kappa\phi_{0}^{-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F+\kappa\phi_{0}^{-1}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F+\kappa\phi_{0}^{-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F_{x},

which, along with (
D.4
), yield that
F_{x}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}
and

(D.5)

F_{xx}=-s\phi_{0}^{-s-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}\mathcal{Q}+\phi_{0}^{-s}\mathcal{Q}_{x}+\kappa\phi_{0}^{-2}((\phi_{0})_{x})^{2}F-\kappa\phi_{0}^{-1}(\phi_{0})_{xx}F-\kappa\phi_{0}^{-1}(\phi_{0})_{x}F_{x}.

Finally, substituting (
D.1
) into (
D.5
), one obtains (
D.3
), which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Coordinate Transformation

In this appendix, we give the transformation relations between
(\rho(t,y),u(t,y),\Gamma(t))
and
(H(t,x),U(t,x))
. First, for every
t
and
y\in I(t)
, define the inverse flow mapping
\tilde{\eta}
by

x=\tilde{\eta}(t,y):\quad I(t)\to I,\quad(t,y)\mapsto(t,x),

and set

(E.1)

\rho(t,y)=\frac{\rho_{0}(\tilde{\eta}(t,y))}{\eta_{x}(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y))},\quad u(t,y)=U(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),\quad\Gamma(t)=\{\eta(t,0),\eta(t,1)\}.

Note that
(\eta,\tilde{\eta})
satisfies the following relations:

(E.2)

\frac{\partial(x,t)}{\partial(y,t)}=\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{x}^{-1}(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y))&-u\eta_{x}^{-1}(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y))\\
\quad\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}.

Then it follows from (
E.1
)-(
E.2
) and the Leibniz formula that

\displaystyle(\rho^{\alpha})_{y}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+1}}-\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+2}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle(\rho^{\alpha})_{t}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+1}}-\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+1}}+\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{xx}U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+2}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle(\rho^{\alpha})_{yy}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+2}}-\frac{(2\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}+\frac{\alpha(\alpha+2)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{xx}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle(\rho^{\alpha})_{ty}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{(\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+2}}-\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+2}}+\frac{\alpha(\alpha+2)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+2}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{(2\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}+\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{\alpha(\alpha+2)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{xx}^{2}U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{3}(\rho^{\alpha})(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial_{x}^{3}\rho_{0}^{\alpha}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{(3\alpha+3)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}-\frac{(3\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{(3\alpha^{2}+9\alpha+3)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}\eta_{xx}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+5}}-\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{4}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{(3\alpha^{2}+7\alpha)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+5}}-\frac{(\alpha^{3}+6\alpha^{2}+8\alpha)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\eta_{xx}^{3}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+6}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

(E.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\rho^{\alpha})_{yy}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(-\frac{(\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{(2\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{3}U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}-\frac{(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}U_{x}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{(2\alpha^{2}+7\alpha+3)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}+\frac{2\alpha(\alpha+2)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U_{xx}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{\alpha(\alpha+3)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}-\frac{\alpha(\alpha+2)(\alpha+4)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U_{x}\eta_{xx}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+5}}-\frac{\partial_{x}^{3}\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+3}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{3(\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{xx}U\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}+\frac{(3\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}+\frac{\alpha\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U\partial_{x}^{4}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+4}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\frac{3(\alpha^{2}+3\alpha+1)(\rho_{0}^{\alpha})_{x}U\eta_{xx}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+5}}-\frac{\alpha(3\alpha+7)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U\eta_{xx}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+5}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{\alpha(\alpha+2)(\alpha+4)\rho_{0}^{\alpha}U\eta_{xx}^{3}}{\eta_{x}^{\alpha+6}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle u_{y}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{U_{x}}{\eta_{x}}(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),\quad u_{t}(t,y)=\left(U_{t}-\frac{UU_{x}}{\eta_{x}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle u_{yy}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{U_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}-\frac{U_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{3}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle u_{ty}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{U_{tx}}{\eta_{x}}-\frac{U_{x}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}-\frac{UU_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{2}}+\frac{UU_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{3}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{3}u(t,y)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial_{x}^{3}U}{\eta_{x}^{3}}-\frac{3U_{xx}\eta_{xx}}{\eta_{x}^{4}}-\frac{U_{x}\partial_{x}^{3}\eta}{\eta_{x}^{4}}+\frac{3U_{x}\eta_{xx}^{2}}{\eta_{x}^{5}}\right)(t,\tilde{\eta}(t,y)),

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\{U(t,0),U(t,1)\},\quad\Gamma^{\prime\prime}(t)=\{U_{t}(t,0),U_{t}(t,1)\}.
